## 0. The rulings this unit derives from (recorded, NOT re-opened) and the go-ahead

**These are binding and are NOT re-opened by this filing.** Where two sources name the unit's contract in different words, **this spec obeys the ruling's own words** and records the discrepancy in `§7a`/`§7a.1`.

| # | Ruling | Where it lands here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | **`A-d3` — the node-local interaction rule** (`docs/decisions.md`'s ACTIVE row `INTERACTION-NODE-LOCAL`; gate record `S-d9`): *all shell interaction mechanisms work through **local handlers on the element that receives the interaction**; document-delegated pointer tracking is REJECTED.* | `§1`, `§2.2` (P-2/P-3), `§2.3`, `§3.2` |
| **2** | **`S-d9`'s narrow capture reading (binding, per `H-r9`):** capture is **`0` calls before the interaction is established**, **PERMITTED after** it, **per-control opt-in** (`capture` default `false`). *An outright ban is rejected because it would make the fork's own `lostpointercapture` criterion inert and would lose the pointer when the cursor leaves the window.* **`Q2` is ANSWERED (a)** (`docs/next-steps.md`'s `Q2` row). | `§2.2` (P-3), `§2.3` item 6, `M-14`/`M-15`, `F-10`, `I-7`/`I-8`, `§5.5.1 P-GS-IM-5` |
| **3** | **The ruled shape, contract-exact (`SCH-2`):** an **injected event source**; each **gesture-start listener attached to ITS OWN CONTROL ELEMENT**; **gesture-scoped tracking listeners opened ONLY between start and end**; **exactly ONE COMMIT per gesture**; **`dispose()` restoring the listener baseline**. | `§2.1`, `§2.3`, `§2.4`, `§2.5`, `§3.1`, `§3.3` |
| **4** | **The REJECTED shapes (`SCH-2`, verbatim):** no document-delegated `pointerdown` · no per-event `closest(selectors)` · no capture at `pointerdown` · no `selectors` vocabulary · no `threshold` vocabulary · no `data-zone`/`pane-collapse-toggle`-style consumer vocabulary. | `§2.2` (P-1..P-3, P-7), `R-1`/`R-2`/`R-7` |
| **5** | **The acceptance criteria (`SCH-2`, verbatim):** **zero** document-delegated listeners · **zero** `closest`/`querySelector` calls · **zero** capture calls **before establishment** · no `selectors`/`threshold`/`data-zone`/`pane-collapse-toggle` vocabulary · **no store, no persistence**. | `§2.2`, `§3.3` (`I-4`..`I-8`, `I-12`), `§3.4` |
| **6** | **`H-r8`'s six prohibitions** apply, judged under the `A-d1`/`S-d8` admission rule's clause **(C)**, with `H-r14`'s clarification that **prohibition 5 bounds UNIT ADOPTION, never this repo's right to add a tool**. | `§2.2` (P-4..P-6, P-8..P-10) |
| **7** | **The mandatory GEOMETRY CLAUSE (the same words `U-ZONES`/`U-CENSUS` carry):** *"the **contract** and its **call counts** are provable here; **any rendered-geometry claim is UNPROVABLE in this repo today**"* — **therefore this unit offers NO `[U]` row**, and **`§2.3` item 5's magnitude clause binds**: what local handlers purchase is **origin REACHABILITY, not magnitude-equivalence**; **no later pass may claim agent-drivable drags** (`S-d9`'s own sentence; `RK-19`'s false-green class). | `§2.3` item 5/7, `§3.4 R-8`, `§3.3 I-11`, `§5.2`, `§6`, `§7` item 5 |
| **8** | **The divergence-harness precondition, NAMED not assumed** (gate record's go/no-go item **3**: *"Until it exists, **no unit may claim `F-1` is proven** or assert a real-DOM attribute row"*). The **structural** half of `F-1` is satisfied here **structurally**; the **behavioural** half needs `U-DIVERGENCE-EXT` (row `C2`). | `§2.6` item 7, `F-11`/`F-12`, `§5.2`, `§7` item 4, `§8` |
| **9** | **No new MCP surface, no store, no persistence, no CSP change, no shim change, no IPC method** (the gate record's *"Adopted units' security / equivalence obligations"*: `ALL_TOOLS` **21**, `RpcMethod` **21**, `MUTATING_METHODS` **7**, `VALID_GROUPS` **5** members; *"It also adds **no IPC method and no MCP tool**, so it must appear in **none** of the six registration sites"*). | `§2.2` (P-6, P-8), `R-6`, `§5.1` |
| **10** | **`U-GSESSION` is NOT reachable by `provident.dispatch`** (the gate record's own obligation row): it is host-side DOM wiring, not a graph node — *"no session handle in `list_targets`; a dispatch on a session-owned element's node yields the ordinary handler result with no session side effect."* | `F-9`, `F-9(b)`, `R-6`, `§2.2` (P-6) |
| **11** | **Isolation (`MULTI-GRAPH-ISOLATION`):** *"the session installs into **one** graph's DOM only and must never reach the isolated `SecurePanels` graph"*. | `M-16`, `F-8`, `R-5`, `§1` item 6 |

### 0A. The dated ruling notes — the clauses the sources leave open, RULED here (2026-09-27)

**What this section is.** The ledger text is **contract-exact** about the lifecycle and the binding negatives; it is **silent** about several clauses a TestWriter must have before it can author a **falsifiable** row. **This filing DECIDES each of those clauses and records the decision here**, each with its reason and its landing site. **No decision here weakens a binding negative**, and **`§7a` lists the items that could NOT be decided from the sources** — those are ARCHITECT questions, and they are the ones `§7a.1` does **not** rule.

**Ruling note 1 — the module path is `src/shared/gesture-session.ts`, and the test file is `tests/gesture-session.test.ts`.** **No source names either path.** The sibling convention decides it (`src/shared/mount-invariant-guard.ts`, `owned-list-host.ts`, `slot-host.ts`, `layout-projection.ts`, `zones.ts`, `census.ts`), and the unit's own name (`U-GSESSION`, *gesture session*) decides the stem. **Nothing else in this file presumes the path.** *(The same note names the test file, because `§5.1`'s diff scope must be a real, checkable allow-list.)*

**Ruling note 2 — THERE IS NO AMBIENT ELEMENT SOURCE AND NO SELECTOR CHANNEL: the CONTROL ELEMENT IS PASSED BY THE CONSUMER.** The ledger's *"each gesture-start listener attached to ITS OWN CONTROL ELEMENT"* is expressed here as an **element argument of `install(element, options)`** — the **exact object reference** the consumer already holds. **The session NEVER derives, looks up or re-queries an element** (`R-2` forbids `closest`/`querySelector`/`getElementById`/`querySelectorAll` **as tokens in any form**), **and it holds no registry of controls other than the ones it installed into** (that registry exists precisely so `dispose()` can perform the baseline restore the ledger names). **The element is compared by IDENTITY (`===`, `Object.is`-equivalent for objects), never by name, class, attribute or position.** Landing: `§2.1`, `§2.4` item 2, `M-1`, `R-2`, `R-5`, **`I-9`**.

**Ruling note 3 — "THE LISTENER BASELINE" IS THE SESSION'S OWN LEDGER, AND `dispose()` RESTORES IT EXACTLY.** *"`dispose()` restoring the listener baseline"* cannot mean *"the element has zero listeners afterwards"*: **the session cannot observe, enumerate or remove listeners another party installed, and a claim that it can would be a false claim about an unverifiable subject.** **RULED:** the session maintains an **exact ledger** of the listeners **it** attached, and `dispose()` **detaches every one of them**, so that **after `dispose()` the session's own contribution to the page is EMPTY** — every `on(element, type, handler)` call it made is matched by exactly one `off(element, type, handler)` call with the same three values. **`dispose()` reports what it did** (`DisposeReport.removed`) **and reports honestly whether the detach was complete** (`DisposeReport.complete`) — because an injected source's `off` may itself fail, and **that failure must not be reported as a baseline restore**. Landing: `§2.1` (`dispose`), `§2.3` item 7, `M-13`, `I-5`, `F-7`, **`P-GS-IM-6`**.

**Ruling note 4 — THE SESSION DOES NOT READ POINTER IDENTITY, AND `pointerup` IS UNCONDITIONALLY TERMINAL.** The ledger requires *"the session ends on up/cancel"* and nothing about multi-pointer disambiguation. **RULED:** the session treats **every** `pointerup` **and every `pointercancel`** as a terminal event for the active gesture, **without reading any field of the event object** (`pointerId`, `button`, `buttons`, `isPrimary` — the event is **opaque and is never inspected**, which is also what keeps `P-GS-TP-2`'s boundary trivially satisfiable and makes the magnitude clause of `§2.3` item 5 airtight). **A second pointer's `pointerdown`** on the same control while a gesture is active is **not** a terminal and does **not** start a second gesture: it is handled by the **first-only rule** (`§2.3` item 3), so a gesture has **exactly one active gesture per session** and **no pointer bookkeeping exists**. **`U-GUTTER`/`U-RELOCATE` own any pointer-identity policy they want, and they may not ask the session for it.** Landing: `§2.4` items 3/4, `M-9`, **`F-9c`**, `§5.5.1 P-GS-TP-2`. **This is the one clause of this filing that COULD be a contract change later; it is recorded as ARCHITECT QUESTION 3 (`§7a`) because the sources are silent and a real multi-pointer consumer could want otherwise.**

**Ruling note 5 — THE CONSUMER NEVER CALLS `commit()`: THE SESSION CALLS IT EXACTLY ONCE, AT A TERMINAL.** The ledger's *"exactly ONE COMMIT per gesture"* and *"cancel ⇒ ZERO commits"* are only **countable** if the session owns the call. **RULED:** `commit` is an **injected callback** that the session invokes **exactly once**, and only from the **`end`** and **`reset`** terminals (**`cancel` invokes it zero times**). **This is what makes `U-GUTTER`'s and `U-RELOCATE`'s commit rows derivable FROM THE SESSION rather than re-implemented per controller** (`§2.6` items 1–3), and it is the mechanism of the single-gesture-authority remedy. **A consumer that wants different writes builds the value in `onMove`/`onEnd` and lets the session commit it.** Landing: `§2.1` (`SessionOptions.commit`), `§2.3` item 5, `§2.6`, `M-10`/`M-11`/`M-12`, `I-2`/`I-3`/**`I-3b`**/`I-3c`, **`P-GS-SM-3`**. **Recorded as ARCHITECT QUESTION 2 (`§7a`) because the alternative shape (a controller-called `session.commit()`) is expressible with the same ledger words.**

**Ruling note 6 — `reset` IS A SESSION TERMINAL, and *"the supplied default"* stays the CALLER'S VALUE.** The session exposes **three** terminals (`end`, `reset`, `cancel`) because `U-GUTTER`'s row — *"reset ⇒ exactly ONE commit of the **supplied** default"* — **cannot be expressed as a sequence of the other two without violating the one-commit-per-gesture rule** (a `cancel` + a fresh `begin` + `end` is **two gestures**, and a bare `end` with a default value is indistinguishable from a user-chosen end). **The session is still policy-free about it:** it accepts **any** caller-supplied value, holds **no** default, and **never computes one** — *"the supplied default"* is the consumer's value and the session merely commits it **exactly once** with the outcome `'reset'` so the consumer's `commit` callback can tell the two terminals apart. Landing: `§2.1` (`reset`), `§2.3` item 5, `M-12`, `I-3b`, `F-4`, `§2.6` item 3. **Recorded as ARCHITECT QUESTION 1 (`§7a`) for the same reason as note 5.**

**Ruling note 7 — THE LEDGER'S `E3`-BEFORE-`E6` ORDER IS AN ANOMALY, AND THIS FILING FOLLOWS THE ARCHITECT'S ORDERING INSTEAD.** `docs/next-steps.md`'s `## OPEN` table numbers `E3` (`U-GUTTER`) **before** `E6` (`U-GSESSION`), and the `E3` row's own `Blocked on` cell names *"then `U-CENSUS` **and `U-GSESSION`** (the session it composes)"* — i.e. **the table's own dependency edge contradicts its row order**. The architect has approved `U-GSESSION` landing **before** `E3`, and the ledger's reasoning is explicit: adopting the controllers is safe **because** this session exists as the single gesture authority (`V-13`'s class). **RULED: this filing lands the session first and states the anomaly rather than silently renumbering another file's rows** (this pass edits no existing file). **No row id, wave letter or queue position is changed by this pass**; the queue rows are the supervisor's to reconcile. Landing: this note, `§0` ruling 14, `§8`.

**Ruling note 8 — THE SESSION'S OWN STATE IS ONE GESTURE DEEP AND IS DISCARDED AT EVERY TERMINAL.** So that *"no store, no persistence"* is a **testable** claim rather than a slogan: the instance keeps (a) the installed-control ledger, (b) **at most one** record for the active gesture — its element, its monotonic id, and its current value — and (c) two monotonic counters (`gestureSeq`, `commitCount`). **Every terminal discards (b) entirely** (`active` becomes `false`, `element`/`value` become `undefined`, `outcome` keeps only the terminal's own word), and **the counters never reset** (they are the *"one commit per gesture"* evidence, `§2.1` `stats()`). **No value, key, element or policy crosses a gesture boundary.** Landing: `§2.3` item 8, `I-12`, `I-12b`, `M-13`, `§2.2` P-8.

**Ruling note 9 — THE EVENT SOURCE IS `{on, off, isConnected?}`, AND AN UNUSABLE SOURCE IS THE VALID-STATE DEGRADATION, NEVER A THROW.** This repo's doctrine is that **environment readings are INJECTED** (`U-LISTHOST`'s `itemFactory`, `U-SLOTHOST`'s element factory per `SLOTHOST-CONTAINER-SOURCE-IS-INJECTED`, `A-d6`'s `{prefersDark}` env), and the `A-d3` ruling's own words are **an injected event source**. **RULED:** the source is an object with the callables `on`, `off` and an **optional** `isConnected`; **if the source (or any of `on`/`off`) is not usable — absent, non-object, non-callable, or THROWING when called — the session degrades to its VALID state:** `install` returns `false`, `begin` reports the declared code, `stats()` reports zeros, and **nothing throws at the consumer boundary** (`createGestureSession` itself never throws, whatever it is handed). **`isConnected` MAY be absent** (a headless/test source need not model connectivity); **it is consulted only when callable, and its `false` is the ONLY failing answer** — a non-boolean return, a throw, or its absence means **"no contrary evidence"** and the check passes. Landing: `§2.1` (`EventSource`), `§2.4` items 5/6, `F-5`..`F-8`, `I-10`/`I-11`, `§5.5.1 P-GS-TP-1`.

**Ruling note 10 — THE EVENT TYPES ARE THE SESSION'S CONTRACT, AND THE CONSUMER NEVER SUPPLIES ONE.** The rejected `selectors` vocabulary is a **selector string**; the event-type names are **not** consumer vocabulary — they are the **mechanism's own contract with its injected source**, and `A-d3`'s own quoted sentence names `pointerdown` itself. **RULED:** the session pins the four `TYPE` constants **privately** (`'pointerdown'`, `'pointermove'`, `'pointerup'`, `'pointercancel'`) and **exposes no parameter, option, union member or default by which a consumer could substitute one** — so **no `selectors` string and no `threshold` number exists anywhere in the surface** (`P-1`, `R-1`). **A consumer that needs different event types does not exist under the `A-d7` static-UI reading, and inventing one would be a new contract with its own gate.** Landing: `§2.1` (the private constants note), `§2.4` item 1, `R-1`, `§4.4 S-1`.

**Ruling note 11 — EVERY TERMINAL DETACHES THE TRACKING LISTENERS **BEFORE** IT RUNS ANY CONSUMER CODE.** The ledger bounds *"gesture-scoped tracking listeners opened **ONLY between start and end**"* at **the gesture**, not at the consumer's callback. **RULED:** the terminal's order is **(1) detach `pointermove`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel`, (2) mark the gesture inactive, (3) run the `onEnd`/`onCancel` hook, (4) invoke `commit` for `end`/`reset`** — so **the listener baseline is already restored before any consumer code runs**, and **a `commit` that throws cannot leave a tracking listener behind** nor prevent the detach. Landing: `§2.3` item 4, `M-10`, `I-4`, `F-3` (**`F-3`'s `commit`-throws half**), `§2.6` item 5.

**Ruling note 12 — THE SESSION EXPORTS A COUNT SEAM (`stats()`), AND IT IS NOT MCP-VISIBLE.** The ledger's count rows are **only falsifiable** if the counts can be read: `stats()` returns the session's own `{installed, sourceCalls, gestures, commits, active, outcome}`, and `gesture()` returns the active-gesture reading. **Both are ordinary module methods** — **no IPC method, no MCP tool, no RPC case, no `list_targets` handle** (`§0` ruling 10, `F-9`). **A later pass that wired `stats()` to an agent-reachable surface would be adding an MCP surface and would need its own gate.** Landing: `§2.1` (`stats`, `gesture`), `§2.3` item 8, `M-15`/`M-16`/`M-17`, `F-9`, `R-6`.

**No item of `§7a` is decided here.** The five `§7a` items are the clauses where **two admissible readings both satisfy the ledger's words and the choice changes a public signature or a consumer obligation**; each is an **ARCHITECT question** with this filing's recommendation and the clause it blocks (`§7a.1`).

## Layer declaration (read this before any table below)

**This spec is DOC-LAYER only.** No leg of it ran in this pass: no suite ran, no trio ran, no Electron window booted, and **no result is recorded here.**

| Label | Layer | What it is | What it is **not** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[T]** | harness-side | the repo's own `tests/**` + `src/shared/dom-shim.ts` (host-owned test code) under the node suite | not a browser, not the assembled app |
| **[H]** | host-side | this repo's `src/**` — here, this unit's own `src/shared/gesture-session.ts` module | not engine-internal behaviour |
| **[U]** | real-DOM `ui` leg | `npm run ui` — the real-Electron observation leg landed by `U-REALDOM-BOOT` | **not** an identity leg; **not** assembled-app acceptance; **NOT OFFERED BY THIS SPEC** (`§5.2`) |
| **[D]** | divergence harness | `npm run divergence` — the shim ≡ real identity leg (`N = 9` pinned) | **the EXTENDED harness (`U-DIVERGENCE-EXT`, row `C2`) does not exist yet**; this spec **claims no `[D]` row** (`§2.6` item 7) |

**Five honesty anchors, carried so no row of this unit over-reads its layer:**

1. **A green node suite is envelope/pure-layer evidence — never assembled-app evidence.** It says this repo's vitest files pass against `src/shared/dom-shim.ts` and against this unit's module. **No window is booted, no IPC round-trip runs, no MCP transport is exercised, and no real DOM is touched.**
2. **This unit touches no DOM directly, at all — not even the shim.** Its rows need **no element from a document**: the **element is an argument**, the **event source is an argument**, and the **listeners are recorded by an argument-supplied recorder**. **A `[T]` green here proves LIFECYCLE and CALL COUNTS only.**
3. **The module reads NO ambient global and performs NO realm access** — no `document`, no `window`, no `globalThis`-rooted lookup, no `matchMedia`, no `getComputedStyle`, no `activeElement`, no `Date`, no `Math.random`, no `process.env`. **Every environment reading is an argument it was handed** (the source object), and it is judged under **(C)'s prohibitions**, which `§2.2` asserts.
4. **The property register (`§5.5.1`) is THIS unit's own property layer and changes nothing above.** Its rows are authored in **this unit's own test file** and executed by the **same node suite** (`npm test`, `§5.2` leg 1) — so **a register row is `[T]` evidence exactly as a `§3` row is**, and **no register row may be read as `[H]`, `[U]`, `[D]` or assembled-app evidence.**
5. **A listener-count green is NOT a listener-removal green in the engine's own retained map.** The gate record's own limit binds here: *"a listener-removal property (the shim removes by reference, and the engine's retained map may hold a different reference)"* — **this unit's rows assert the CALLS the session made against the injected source, never a fact about a real browser's listener table.**

## 1. Scope

**One deliverable: one `src/shared` module — a node-local interaction session with an INJECTED event source, one active gesture at a time, exactly one commit per gesture, and a `dispose()` that restores its own listener baseline.**

1. **What the unit is, in one sentence.** A **synchronous, policy-free gesture lifecycle**: the consumer hands it an **event source** and, per control element, a small **config**; the session attaches **one** gesture-start listener to **that element**, opens the gesture-scoped tracking listeners **only between start and terminal**, carries the gesture's **current value** (whatever the consumer's own `onMove` computed), and **commits exactly once** at a terminal — `end` (one commit), `reset` (exactly one commit of the **supplied** value), `cancel` (**zero** commits).
2. **What it is NOT — no policy.** **Bounds, defaults, resizability, axes, thresholds, drop resolution, candidates and reveal sets are ALL the consumer's**: the session **computes no value**, holds **no default**, compares **no** pointer position, evaluates **no** policy predicate, and **reads NO field of any event object**. This is what makes `U-GUTTER`'s and `U-RELOCATE`'s arithmetic/policy rows **their own** and this unit's rows **counts and lifecycle** (§2.6).
3. **What it is NOT — no DOM access.** The session **never** touches a DOM global: it calls **only** the injected source's `on`/`off`/`isConnected` (and only for elements and handlers it was handed), and it **never** looks an element up by selector, id, class, attribute or name (`R-2`).
4. **What it is NOT — no UI.** It authors **no text, no control, no affordance, no class, no attribute and no styling**; it is a **mechanism**, and outside the `UI-RENDERED-WITH-PROVIDENT` constraint **because it is not a UI element** (`SHELL-CHROME-CARVE-OUT-FUNCTIONAL`, `§0` ruling 6).
5. **What it is NOT — no store, no persistence, no journal, no MCP surface.** No file, no `localStorage`, no store object, no cache, no module-level mutable state, no new tool/resource/group/`VALID_GROUPS` member/`RpcMethod` member/`MUTATING_METHODS` entry/IPC method. **A session is NOT journaled**, so **no `undo()`/`replay()` inverse exists** and **no unit may claim magnitude-equivalence with `provident.op`'s `state-slice`** (`§0` ruling 7).
6. **What it is NOT — no cross-unit composition, no duplication.** It **imports nothing** (not `U-ZONES`, not `U-CENSUS`, not `U-PROJ`, not `U-LISTHOST`/`U-SLOTHOST`, not the shim, not the engine) and **duplicates no sibling responsibility**. **`U-GUTTER` and `U-RELOCATE` compose THIS unit; this unit composes nothing** (`§0` ruling 13).
7. **Two dependencies EXPLICITLY ABSENT, recorded so a later pass does not assume them.** **(a)** The engine pin (`A-d2`) is **SPENT**: this module imports nothing and exercises no engine surface. **(b)** Neither `DONE` sibling (`U-ZONES` row `E1`, `U-CENSUS` row `E2`) is a dependency: **no edge exists in either direction** — a later pass asserting one would be a **fabricated edge** (`H-r6`'s dissolved-edge class).
8. **What is EXPLICITLY OUT of scope (do not do in this unit).** No `createResizeController`/`clampToBounds`/`boundsFor`/`defaultSizeFor`/`isResizable`/`axisFor` (that is `U-GUTTER`, `E3`). No `createRelocateSession`/`candidatesFor`/`resolveTarget`/`onReveal`/drop model/`threshold` (that is `U-RELOCATE`, `E4`). No `tokensFor`/`orientationFor`/`contain` declaration (`U-CONTAINER`, `E5`). No theme/`resolveTheme` (`U-THEME`, `E8`). No menu catalog (`U-MENULIB`, `E7`). No overlay state machine (`U-OVERLAY`, `E9`). No mount cardinality (`U-MOUNTGUARD`, `DONE`). No census/track vocabulary (`U-ZONES`/`U-CENSUS`, both `DONE`). No projection/applier/write sink (`U-PROJ`, `DONE`). No list host or slot host (`U-LISTHOST`/`U-SLOTHOST`, both `DONE`). No new MCP surface. No shim change. No `docs/skills/designing-pages.md` update — **no such file exists** (globbed `docs/skills/*` this pass: `process-guardrails.md` alone), so there is **no test-use-case coverage matrix and no demo-page index to update**, and **this unit renders no page**.
9. **What the unit may land.** The module (NEW) + its red/green rows + the register rows + this spec. **No host change**: this unit adds one new `src/shared/` module and touches **no existing file** except this spec and the trackers (`§5.1`).

## 2. The surface (exact)

### 2.1 What this unit ADDS — every exported name, signature, return shape, and error pattern

**New module: `src/shared/gesture-session.ts`** (`§0A` note 1). **It imports NOTHING — not even a type** (`R-4`).

**EXPORT CENSUS — stated before the block, and it MUST AGREE with the block: TWELVE exported names, in TWO halves — FOUR value exports and EIGHT type declarations.** **The two halves are counted separately on purpose**, because sibling reviews have caught a census cell that contradicted the block beside it, and because a **type declaration is erased at runtime** — so a single "12 exports" claim would be **unfalsifiable at runtime by half**. **This block declares exactly `4 + 8 = 12` exported names and nothing else**, and **`R-6` is the row that pins the SET (not the count)**: **(a) the runtime value exports — exactly `createGestureSession`, `installGestureListeners`, `detachGestureListeners` and `POINTER_TYPES`** (`R-6(a)` reads the imported namespace's own keys, with a positive control that a namespace carrying a fifth value export **FAILS**); **(b) the TYPE-ONLY names — exactly `EventSource`, `GestureElement`, `GestureHandle`, `GestureOptions`, `GestureOptionsInput`, `SessionOptions`, `SessionStats`, `GestureStats`** (`R-6(b)`, a **compile-time** claim covered by `§5.2` leg 4). **A row asserting only a COUNT without NAMING the names FAILS `R-6`'s own text** (`§4.4 S-2`).

```ts
/** WHAT `isConnected` MAY DO — total, and the ONLY connectivity reading the
 *  session is allowed to consult. It is consulted ONCE per `begin` attempt.
 *  ABSENT / non-callable ⇒ "no contrary evidence".  A THROW ⇒ "no contrary
 *  evidence" (the failure is swallowed, never propagated).  Returning exactly
 *  `false` is the ONLY answer that makes connectivity fail; any other return
 *  (including `undefined`, `null`, `0`, `''`) means "not known to be
 *  disconnected" and the check PASSES (§2.4 item 5). */
export type GestureElement = unknown

/** THE INJECTED EVENT SOURCE — the A-d3 seam. The session NEVER calls
 *  `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener`, `setPointerCapture`,
 *  `releasePointerCapture`, `closest`, `querySelector*` or `getElementById`
 *  on anything, ever (§2.2 P-2/P-3; R-1/R-2/R-7).  Every listener the session
 *  attaches goes through `on` and every detach through `off`, with the EXACT
 *  three values it was given (`R-7`). */
export interface EventSource {
  /** Attach `handler` to `element` for the event TYPE named (one of the
   *  session's four private constants).  Called EXACTLY once per attached
   *  listener; never called for a handler already attached.  A source whose
   *  `on` is not callable, or throws, makes the session degrade to its VALID
   *  state (§2.4 item 6) — the throw never reaches the consumer. */
  on(element: GestureElement, type: string, handler: () => void): void
  /** Detach `handler` from `element` for that TYPE.  Called EXACTLY once per
   *  prior `on` call, with the same three values (referential identity for
   *  `handler`).  A throwing `off` is caught PER CALL: the remaining detaches
   *  still run, and the report's `complete` becomes `false` (`§0A` note 3). */
  off(element: GestureElement, type: string, handler: () => void): void
  /** OPTIONAL. The ONE connectivity reading allowed (`§2.4` item 5). */
  isConnected?(element: GestureElement): boolean
  /** OPTIONAL — **THE CAPTURE CAPABILITY, DECLARED (`⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27`, the `D-1`/`ADV-GS-3` amendment: the member is read by this fixed name from the injected source, so the DOCUMENTED interface declares it — a consumer implementing a source typed `EventSource` can now opt in with a TYPE-LEGAL source, and the member's NAME is derivable from `§2.5` item 2).** It is invoked **AT MOST ONCE per gesture**, **only inside `begin`**, **only after the gesture is established** (after the three tracking attaches, `§2.3` item 6(b)), and **only for a control that opted in via `capture: true`**. **Its ABSENCE IS THE DECLARED DEGRADATION** — **ZERO capture calls, no throw and no diagnostic needed**, and **the gesture still establishes and still terminates normally** (`I-7`; the row is **`M-8b`**). **A throw from it is swallowed by the same valid-state rule as the source's other callables** (`§2.4` item 6) — a throwing capture member never reaches the consumer boundary and never fails a gesture. **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (`ADV-GS-17`): MEASURED, THE MODULE'S OWN `EventSource` DECLARES THIS MEMBER — the declaration has LANDED (`src/shared/gesture-session.ts`), leg 4 is `0` diagnostics, and the red→green cycle is complete (the red was `TS2353`). No `D-1` module-layer remedy remains.** */
  capturePointer?(element: GestureElement): void
}

/** WHAT THE CONSUMER PASSES AS THE PER-CONTROL OPTIONS.  Both fields are
 *  OPTIONAL; an absent `capture` is `false` (the A-d3 pin, §0 ruling 2), and
 *  an absent field is THE ABSENCE OF OPT-IN, never a mechanism default.
 *  `capture` is read for TRUTHINESS (`Boolean(capture) === true`), so any
 *  truthy value opts in and every falsy value does not (P-GS-IM-5). */
export interface GestureOptionsInput {
  /** PER-CONTROL OPT-IN for pointer capture AFTER establishment.  `false`
   *  (or absent, or falsy of any kind) ⇒ ZERO capture calls, ever.  `true`
   *  (or any truthy value) ⇒ exactly ONE **capture call through the INJECTED SOURCE'S OWN CAPTURE METHOD** — 
 *  **⟶ RULED 2026-09-27 (the red-set/green-set conflict the Implementer stopped on; the resolution is the one that satisfies BOTH clauses):** the session calls a method the SOURCE supplies (named by the source, discovered by the session from its own supplied surface) and the MODULE'S SOURCE TEXT carries **no DOM-specific capture method token** — because `EventSource`'s own doc block forbids the session from calling `setPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture` on anything, ever, and the module-level scans (`R-3`(2), `F-11`) fail any module whose bytes contain that token in any form. **The capture requirement and the token prohibition are therefore BOTH satisfied by injection: the capability is the caller's, the invocation is the session's, and the count (`exactly ONE` on an opted-in establishment, `ZERO` otherwise) is unchanged.** No id, term, strategy id or the `396` total moves.
   *  injected source, inside `begin`, AFTER the gesture is established. */
  readonly capture?: unknown
  /** Called synchronously at the END of a successful `begin`, AFTER the
   *  tracking listeners are attached and AFTER the capture call (if opted in).
   *  It receives the element and the gesture handle.  A throw propagates to the
   *  caller of `begin` and leaves NO listener behind (`§2.3` item 3).  **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the
   *  `ADV-GS-16` amendment; the as-filed sentence above is kept visible): a throw from here ALSO
   *  means the `begin` was NOT SUCCESSFUL — the gesture counter and the handle id are NOT
   *  consumed, `gesture()` stays `null`, and the element stays installed and re-establishable
   *  (`§2.3` item 1(d)). */
  readonly onStart?: unknown
  /** Called synchronously for EVERY `pointermove` of the active gesture, with
   *  the gesture handle.  The consumer COMPUTES its own next value here
   *  (bounds, axes, thresholds, resolution — all policy, §1 item 2) and hands
   *  it back with `gesture.set(value)`.  The event object is NOT passed: the
   *  session does not own the event data and does not read any field of it. */
  readonly onMove?: unknown
  /** Called synchronously at an `end` or `reset` terminal, AFTER the tracking
   *  listeners are detached and BEFORE `commit` is invoked (§0A note 11).
   *  `value` is the gesture's current value (`undefined` if the consumer never
   *  set one — the commit still happens exactly once, `§2.3` item 5). */
  readonly onEnd?: unknown
  /** Called synchronously at a `cancel` terminal (including a
   *  `pointercancel`, a cancelled `dispose()`, and a source-disconnect
   *  observation), AFTER detach, BEFORE nothing — `commit` is NEVER invoked
   *  from here. */
  readonly onCancel?: unknown
}

/** THE SESSION'S OWN VIEW OF ONE CONTROL — `capture` already resolved to a
 *  boolean, hooks present only where the consumer supplied a callable. */
export interface GestureOptions {
  readonly capture: boolean
  readonly onStart?: (element: GestureElement) => void
  readonly onMove?: (gesture: GestureHandle) => void
  readonly onEnd?: (element: GestureElement, value: unknown) => void
  readonly onCancel?: (element: GestureElement) => void
}

/** THE HANDLE FOR THE ONE ACTIVE GESTURE.  Handed to `onStart`/`onMove`, and
 *  accepted (and RE-CHECKED by identity) by every terminal call. */
export interface GestureHandle {
  /** MONOTONIC, instance-local, starts at `1`, increments on every successful
   *  `begin`, NEVER reused and NEVER reset (not even by `dispose()`). */
  readonly id: number
  /** The EXACT element reference passed to `install` (`§0A` note 2). */
  readonly element: GestureElement
  /** `true` while the gesture is in flight (between a successful `begin` and
   *  its terminal); `false` once a terminal has run. */
  readonly active: boolean
  /** `null` while active; then `'end'` · `'reset'` · `'cancel'` — the closed
   *  three-member union, and the ONLY outcome vocabulary the session has.
   *  `'reset'` is how a consumer's `commit` callback distinguishes a reset
   *  from a user-chosen end (§0A note 6). */
  readonly outcome: 'end' | 'reset' | 'cancel' | null
  /** The gesture's CURRENT value — the last value handed to `set`, else
   *  `undefined`.  It is the value `commit` will receive at an `end`/`reset`
   *  terminal, and it is DISCARDED at every terminal (§0A note 8). */
  readonly value: unknown
  /** Set (or replace) the gesture's current value. TOTAL: ANY value is
   *  accepted — `undefined`, `null`, a number, a string, an object, a
   *  function, a `Symbol` — and NOTHING is validated, coerced or defaulted
   *  (§1 item 2).  Returns the handle.  A call on a non-active handle is a
   *  no-op that returns the same handle. */
  set(value: unknown): GestureHandle
}

/** WHAT THE CONSUMER PASSES TO `createGestureSession`.  `commit` is the ONE
 *  injected commit callback (§0A note 5). */
export interface SessionOptions {
  /** THE injected event source (`§2.4` item 6).  Absent / non-object / a
   *  source whose `on` or `off` is not callable ⇒ the valid-state
   *  degradation; `createGestureSession` still returns a usable instance. */
  readonly source?: EventSource
  /** INVOKED BY THE SESSION, EXACTLY ONCE PER GESTURE, and only at an
   *  `end` or `reset` terminal — `cancel` invokes it ZERO times.  It receives
   *  the terminal handle (whose `outcome` distinguishes `'end'` from
   *  `'reset'`) and the gesture's final value.  It is invoked AFTER the
   *  tracking listeners are detached (§0A note 11).  A throw PROPAGATES to
   *  the caller of the terminal; the commit still counts as the gesture's ONE
   *  commit, and nothing is retried (`I-3c`). */
  readonly commit?: (gesture: GestureHandle, value: unknown) => void
}

/** THE RESULT OF `begin`.  A discriminated union: `ok: true` guarantees a
 *  handle, `ok: false` guarantees a `code` and NO handle. */
export type BeginResult =
  | { readonly ok: true; readonly gesture: GestureHandle }
  | { readonly ok: false; readonly code: GestureCode }

/** THE RESULT OF `end` / `reset` / `cancel`.  `committed` is the SESSION'S OWN
 *  record of whether it invoked `commit` for THAT call — `true` for a
 *  successful `end` and a successful `reset`, `false` for a successful
 *  `cancel` and for every refusal.  **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-22`(a) amendment; the
 *  as-filed sentence above is kept visible): THE SENTENCE ABOVE IS SUPERSEDED AS THE
 *  DEFINITION.**  `committed` is the **COMMITTING-TERMINAL DISCRIMINATOR** — `true` for an
 *  `end` and for a `reset`, `false` for a `cancel` and for every refusal — and it is
 *  **INDEPENDENT of whether a `commit` callback was installed**: a session constructed
 *  with NO `commit` callback answers `{ok:true, code:'ok', committed:true}` at `end`/`reset`
 *  while `stats().commits` stays `0`.  The MODULE NEEDS NO CHANGE for this reading; the
 *  TestWriter's row pins it. */
export type TerminalResult = {
  readonly ok: boolean
  readonly code: GestureCode
  readonly committed: boolean
}

/** THE RESULT OF `dispose()`.  `removed` counts the listeners DETACHED BY THIS
 *  CALL; `complete` is `true` iff every detach this call attempted succeeded
 *  (`§0A` note 3).  A second `dispose()` reports `removed: 0`. */
export type DisposeReport = {
  readonly removed: number
  readonly complete: boolean
}

/** THE CLOSED RESULT-CODE UNION — SEVEN members, no eighth (§2.3 item 4). */
export type GestureCode =
  | 'ok'
  | 'not-installed'
  | 'busy'
  | 'disposed'
  | 'disconnected'
  | 'stale'
  | 'no-gesture'

/** THE SESSION'S OWN COUNTERS (`§0A` note 12).  All values are monotonic
 *  except `active`/`outcome`, and nothing here is a policy fact. */
export interface SessionStats {
  /** Installed control elements, by identity. */
  readonly installed: number
  /** Total source calls the session made: `on` + `off` + `isConnected`.
   *  ⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `D-7`/`ADV-GS-7` amendment; the sentence above
   *  is kept): THE EXACT COUNTING RULE, measured from the module — the counter
   *  increments by ONE for a call that RETURNS, and a call that THROWS is NOT
   *  counted.  So `sourceCalls` counts the `on`/`off`/`isConnected` calls that
   *  COMPLETED (an `on` that threw increments nothing and returns `false`, an
   *  `off` that threw increments nothing and is the `complete: false` limb, and
   *  a throwing `isConnected` is swallowed and NOT counted), while a
   *  `capturePointer` call is NEVER counted here (it is not one of the three
   *  members this field names).  A row asserting `sourceCalls` against a
   *  recorded call log must therefore compare it to the count of NON-THROWING
   *  entries. */
  readonly sourceCalls: number
  /** Successful `begin` calls, instance-lifetime.  **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-16`
   *  amendment; the as-filed sentence above is kept visible): a `begin` whose `onStart` hook
   *  THROWS is NOT a successful `begin` — the counter is NOT consumed (it stays one below the
   *  throw), `gesture()` stays `null`, the handle id is not spent, and the element stays
   *  INSTALLED and re-establishable (`§2.3` item 1(d)).  Refused `begin`s consume nothing
   *  either (`ADV-GS-15`). */
  readonly gestures: number
  /** `commit` invocations, instance-lifetime.  **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-22`(a)
   *  amendment; the as-filed sentence above is kept visible): this counter counts the session's
   *  ACTUAL invocations of the injected `commit` callback, so it stays `0` when NO callback was
   *  installed — it is NOT the `committed` field of `TerminalResult`, which is the
   *  committing-terminal discriminator and is `true` at `end`/`reset` regardless of whether a
   *  callback exists (`§2.1`'s `TerminalResult`). */
  readonly commits: number
  readonly active: boolean
  /** The active gesture id, or `0` when idle.  **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-16`
   *  amendment; the as-filed sentence above is kept visible): the id is consumed ONLY by a
   *  SUCCESSFUL `begin` — a `begin` whose `onStart` hook THROWS does NOT spend it, so the
   *  retry establishes with the SAME id the throw attempted (`§2.3` item 1(d)). */
  readonly gestureId: number
  /** The LAST result code this session produced (`'ok'` before anything
   *  happened), so a consumer can read a refusal without a `try`/`catch`. */
  readonly lastCode: GestureCode
}

/** THE ACTIVE-GESTURE READING (`gesture()`). */
export interface GestureStats {
  readonly active: boolean
  readonly id: number
  readonly outcome: 'end' | 'reset' | 'cancel' | null
  readonly value: unknown
  /** `commit` invocations attributable to THIS gesture: `0` while it is
   *  active and after a `cancel`; `1` after an `end`/`reset`.  **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (the
   *  `ADV-GS-35` amendment — ANNOTATE-NEVER-REWRITE, so the AS-FILED sentence above is kept
   *  visible: IT ASKS FOR A READING THE SURFACE CANNOT PRODUCE).**  **MEASURED: a terminal
   *  DISCARDS the active record, so `gesture()` returns `null` after an `end`, a `reset` and a
   *  `cancel` (`§2.1`'s `gesture()`, `§2.4` item 8, `M-17`) — this field is therefore
   *  OBSERVABLE ONLY WHILE A GESTURE IS ACTIVE, where its value is `0`.  No row may read it
   *  after a terminal.**  **THE OBSERVABLE READING OF THE COMMIT COUNT IS `stats().commits`
   *  (the session's own monotonic counter, `commit` invocations instance-lifetime) READ
   *  TOGETHER WITH the injected `commit` sink's own call record and the terminal handle's
   *  `outcome` — which is exactly what `§0A` note 12's count seam is for and what the LANDED
   *  `M-4` row reads (`stats().commits` + the commit sink + `handle.outcome`, never
   *  `gesture().commits`).  This is the same ruling already recorded at `§5.5.1 P-GS-SM-3`
   *  (`ADV-GS-22`(b)).** */
  readonly commits: number
}

/** THE SESSION — the node-local lifecycle.  Every member is TOTAL (`I-10`):
 *  none throws for any argument, whatever the injected source does. */
export interface GestureSession {
  /** Attach ONE `pointerdown` listener to `element`, bound to a new gesture.
   *  Returns `true` iff a listener was attached by THIS call.  Returns
   *  `false` — attaching NOTHING — when: the session is disposed; `element`
   *  is `null`/`undefined`; `element` is ALREADY installed (repeat install is
   *  a NO-OP, never a second listener: the FIRST config wins, §2.4 item 2);
   *  the source is unusable; or the source's `on` threw.  TOTAL: never throws. */
  install(element: GestureElement, options?: GestureOptionsInput): boolean
  /** Establish a gesture on an INSTALLED element.  Called BY THE SESSION's own
   *  `pointerdown` listener; callable by the consumer directly (it is the
   *  seam `R-7`'s tests drive).  Codes: `'ok'` · `'not-installed'` (never
   *  installed, including an element installed only by ANOTHER session) ·
   *  `'busy'` (a gesture is already active on THIS session — one at a time,
   *  `§2.3` item 3) · `'disconnected'` (the source's `isConnected(element)`
   *  returned exactly `false`, `§2.4` item 5) · `'disposed'`.  On `'ok'`: the
   *  tracking listeners are attached, the capture call is made iff opted in,
   *  `onStart` runs, and the handle is returned. */
  begin(element: GestureElement): BeginResult
  /** END the gesture and COMMIT — exactly once — the gesture's current value,
   *  or `value` when a non-`undefined` `value` argument is supplied (§2.4
   *  item 4).  The handle must be THE ACTIVE gesture's handle (identity
   *  checked): a stale handle refuses `'stale'` and commits NOTHING. */
  end(element: GestureElement, gesture: GestureHandle, value?: unknown): TerminalResult
  /** RESET the gesture: detach, then COMMIT — exactly once — the SUPPLIED
   *  value, with `outcome = 'reset'` (§0A note 6).  The session supplies no
   *  default of its own; ANY value is accepted. */
  reset(element: GestureElement, gesture: GestureHandle, value: unknown): TerminalResult
  /** CANCEL the gesture: detach, run `onCancel`, and invoke `commit` ZERO
   *  times.  This is the path for a `pointercancel`, a consumer abort, and a
   *  `dispose()` during a gesture (§2.3 item 6). */
  cancel(element: GestureElement, gesture?: GestureHandle): TerminalResult
  /** THE BASELINE RESTORE (§0A note 3): cancel any active gesture WITHOUT
   *  committing, detach EVERY listener this session attached (start listeners
   *  included), empty the installed ledger, and make the instance
   *  PERMANENTLY INERT.  IDEMPOTENT: a second call is a no-op reporting
   *  `{removed: 0, complete: true}`.  Never throws. */
  dispose(): DisposeReport
  /** The active gesture's reading, or `null` when idle. */
  gesture(): GestureStats | null
  /** The session's own counters. Never throws. */
  stats(): SessionStats
  /** `true` forever once `dispose()` has run. */
  readonly disposed: boolean
}

/** THE FACTORY.  TOTAL: NEVER THROWS, for ANY argument — including a hostile
 *  options object, a Proxy, a primitive, or `undefined`.  An unusable source
 *  yields a usable, inert-until-usable instance (`§2.4` item 6). */
export function createGestureSession(options?: SessionOptions): GestureSession

/** Attach the gesture-start listener for ONE element.  Separately exported so
 *  the session-scoped-tracking contract and the capture-eligibility contract
 *  can each be driven without a session, and so `R-7` has a named seam.
 *  Returns the handler it attached, or `null` when the source is unusable or
 *  `element` is `null`/`undefined`.  TOTAL: never throws. */
export function installGestureListeners(
  source: EventSource | undefined,
  element: GestureElement,
  onStart: (element: GestureElement) => void,
): (() => void) | null

/** Detach a handler previously attached by `installGestureListeners`.
 *  Returns `true` iff the source's `off` was callable and returned without
 *  throwing; `false` for a non-callable source, a non-callable handler, or a
 *  THROWING `off`.  ⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `D-3`(a)/`GS-G-50` amendment;
 *  the as-filed sentence claimed `false` "for an already-detached … case"): the
 *  helper is handed `(source, element, handler)` and NOTHING else, so it cannot
 *  know that a previous call already detached the same triple — the session's
 *  own ledger does that, and the repeat-detach case belongs to `dispose()`
 *  (`§2.3` item 7.5), never to this standalone function.  A repeat detach
 *  therefore returns `true` and reaches the source a second time.  TOTAL:
 *  never throws. */
export function detachGestureListeners(
  source: EventSource | undefined,
  element: GestureElement,
  handler: (() => void) | null,
): boolean

/** THE FOUR EVENT TYPE NAMES the session contracts with its source (§0A
 *  note 10).  Frozen, not configurable, and carrying NO consumer vocabulary:
 *  a selector string, a threshold, a zone/pane/tab name or a
 *  `data-zone`/`pane-collapse-toggle` attribute name appears NOWHERE (P-1).
 *  Separate `installGestureListeners`/`detachGestureListeners` exports exist
 *  so a source can be driven without a session (`R-3`'s named seam). */
export const POINTER_TYPES: Readonly<
  Record<'start' | 'move' | 'end' | 'cancel', string>
>
```

**⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `D-5`/`ADV-GS-5` amendment — FIVE non-exported declarations, MEASURED against the module's bytes; the census itself is UNCHANGED).** **FIVE of the type declarations in the block above carry an `export` keyword the MODULE does not have: `BeginResult`, `TerminalResult`, `DisposeReport`, `GestureCode` and `GestureSession`.** **MEASURED this pass by reading `src/shared/gesture-session.ts`'s own bytes (not inferred): each of those five is declared WITHOUT the keyword — `type GestureCode = 'ok' | … | 'no-gesture'` · `type BeginResult = …` · `type TerminalResult = { … }` · `type DisposeReport = { … }` · `interface GestureSession { … }` (they sit beside the module's other internal declarations, `GestureOutcome` and `ControlEntry`) — while the module's EIGHT `export`-prefixed type declarations are exactly `GestureElement`, `EventSource`, `GestureOptionsInput`, `GestureOptions`, `GestureHandle`, `SessionOptions`, `SessionStats` and `GestureStats`.** **The illustrative block above is therefore a DOC-LEVEL listing of the contract's shapes, NOT the module's export list: the five keywords annotate WHAT THE SESSION'S OWN SIGNATURES RETURN AND ACCEPT, and no pass may add an export to the module to make this listing agree.** **THE EXPORT CENSUS DOES NOT MOVE: `TWELVE` exported names in two halves — FOUR value exports and EIGHT type declarations (`4 + 8`).** `R-6(a)` remains the SET row for the value half; **`R-6(b)` is RE-STATED as a PRESENCE claim at `§3.4`**, because a type-only name is erased at run time and an `EXACTLY` over an erased set is not falsifiable at the type layer. **No register row, id, attempt term, the `396` total, the seed, the caps, the strategy ids, the `12`-name census or any `§2.5` list entry's ORDER moves for this annotation.**

**The four private type constants, pinned exactly** (the session's contract with its injected source; `R-1` scans for the ABSENCE of every other form, `R-7` for the delegation): **`start = 'pointerdown'`** · **`move = 'pointermove'`** · **`end = 'pointerup'`** · **`cancel = 'pointercancel'`**.

**THE DELEGATE CLAUSE — stated exactly, because `U-GUTTER` (`E3`) and `U-RELOCATE` (`E4`) will be written against it and re-opening it later is expensive.**

1. **What they may call, and nothing else:** `createGestureSession({source, commit})` **once per session**; `install(element, {capture?, onStart?, onMove?, onEnd?, onCancel?})` **once per control element**; `begin(element)` **to establish** (normally left to the session's own listener); `end(element, gesture, value?)` · `reset(element, gesture, value)` · `cancel(element, gesture?)` **as terminals**; `dispose()` **to restore the baseline**; `gesture()`/`stats()`/`disposed` **to read state**; and — on the handle the session gave them — `set(value)`.
2. **What they receive:** a **`BeginResult`** (`{ok:true, gesture}` or `{ok:false, code}`), a **`TerminalResult`** (`{ok, code, committed}`), a **`DisposeReport`** (`{removed, complete}`), a **`GestureHandle`**, **`SessionStats`/`GestureStats`**, and — at their own `commit` callback — **(the handle, the final value)**.
3. **What they may NOT do, because it would create the second authority `V-13` names:** attach a listener of their own to the control's `pointerdown`/`pointermove`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel` (the session's listeners are the gesture stream); call their `commit` callback themselves (the session invokes it exactly once — `§0A` note 5); read or set a value except through `gesture.set(...)`; keep a reference to the handle past the terminal (the handle reports `active: false` and every terminal on it refuses `'stale'`); or ask the session for bounds, an axis, a threshold, a drop target or a reveal set (**no such parameter exists**).
4. **What remains THEIRS, and is not expressible through this session:** every policy decision (`clampToBounds`, `defaultSizeFor`, `isResizable`, `axisFor`, `resolveTarget`, `candidatesFor`, `threshold` arithmetic, the reveal set) and **every write to a sink** — including `U-RELOCATE`'s `onReveal`, which **must be invoked only inside its own `commit` callback** if the *"reveal written exactly once per gesture at gesture end"* row is to be derivable (`§2.6` item 7).
5. **The composition's own rows are NOT this spec's rows.** `U-GUTTER`'s call counts and `U-RELOCATE`'s reveal-once row are **their specs'**, and **this filing asserts only that each is DERIVABLE from `§2.3`/`§2.6`** — not that it holds.

### 2.2 What is CALLER-SUPPLIED, and the prohibitions — **every prohibition has a row**

**Caller-supplied (never built in, never defaulted, never enumerated):** the **event source**; the **control element**; the **per-control `capture` opt-in**; the **four hooks** *(`⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27, THE GATE-7 PROOFREADER PASS: this cell AS FILED read "the **five hooks**", and the as-filed word is kept visible — the contract defines FOUR hook inputs, `onStart` · `onMove` · `onEnd` · `onCancel` (`§2.1`'s `GestureOptionsInput` and `GestureOptions`, the landed module's own `GestureOptions` interface, and `F-1`'s *"the session reads only `capture` and the five hooks"* cell); the `capture` opt-in is LISTED SEPARATELY in this very sentence, so the `five` counted it twice. NO hook was added or removed by this correction and no other count in this file is affected.)*; **the `commit` callback**; and **every gesture VALUE** (the session never computes, coerces, rounds, clamps or validates one). **The session contains NO application string, NO zone/pane/tab/selector/threshold literal, NO `data-*` name, NO default value and NO policy predicate.**

| # | Prohibition (source) | How THIS unit satisfies it | Pinned by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **P-1** | **No consumer vocabulary, and no `selectors`/`threshold` vocabulary** (`SCH-2`'s acceptance; `A-d3`; `H-r8` (C)#1): no selector string, no zone/pane/tab/region/dashboard name, no `data-zone`/`pane-collapse-toggle`-style attribute name, no threshold number, no union member, **no documented default** | **The only strings the module owns are its four event-type constants and its seven result codes** (`§2.1`), all of which are **mechanism-internal contract**; **every other string is caller data**; **no selector is ever evaluated and no `threshold` is ever compared** | **`R-1`** (the anti-evasion vocabulary scan), **`R-2`**, `M-3`, `F-1`, `§4.4 S-1` |
| **P-2** | **No DOM access and no global element lookup** (`A-d3`: *"never references a global element lookup, `document`, `window`, …"*; `SCH-2`: **zero** `closest`/`querySelector` calls) | **The module contains no `document`/`window`/`globalThis`-rooted access and no element-query token in ANY form** (raw, assembled, commented or realm-rooted); the element is an **argument** (`§0A` note 2) | **`R-2`**, **`R-7`**, `I-6`, `§4.4 S-1` |
| **P-3** | **No listener outside the injected source** (`A-d3`: no `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', …)`; `SCH-2`: **zero** document-delegated listeners; **zero** capture calls before establishment) | **Every attach/detach/capture goes through the source**, and the **capture call exists only inside `begin`, after the gesture is established**; the module contains no `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener`/`setPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture` token at all | **`R-7`**, **`R-3`**, `M-14`, `I-7`/`I-8`, **`P-GS-IM-5`** |
| **P-4** | **No app UI content** (`H-r8` (C)#2) | The unit authors **no element, no text, no class, no attribute, no stylesheet and no control**; it returns values and calls callbacks — and **the WRITE TOKENS that would author any of them are enumerated and scanned for by the rule row this prohibition now cites** (`⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27`, the `D-9`/`ADV-GS-12` amendment: `R-12`, a static prohibition scan for `setAttribute`/`removeAttribute`/`classList`/`className`/`textContent`/`innerText`/`innerHTML`/`outerHTML`/`insertAdjacent*`/`createElement`/`appendChild`-family and `style`-write tokens — so this prohibition has a row that can FAIL, and `R-8`'s class-write ban is no longer a text with no implemented rule) | **`R-12`**, **`R-1`**, `§7` item 8 |
| **P-5** | **No policy defaults** (`H-r8` (C)#3; `A-d3`: *"the host attaches LOCAL handlers and holds no policy"*) | **There is no default value, no default bound, no default threshold and no inferred decision anywhere**: `capture`'s absent state is **the absence of opt-in** (not a mechanism default), and every value is caller-supplied | **`R-1`**, `M-3`, `I-12`, `§7` item 7 |
| **P-6** | **No new MCP surface** (`H-r8` (C)#5; `H-r14`; the gate record: **none of the six registration sites**) | The module registers nothing and is imported by no `src/**` file; `ALL_TOOLS`/`RpcMethod`/`MUTATING_METHODS`/`VALID_GROUPS` are unchanged; **no IPC method, no tool, no resource, no `RpcMethod` member** | **`R-3`**, **`R-6(c)`**, `F-9`, `F-9(b)`, `§5.1` |
| **P-7** | **The A-d3 REJECTED SHAPES, named explicitly:** no document-delegated `pointerdown` · no per-event `closest(selectors)` · no capture at `pointerdown` · no `selectors` vocabulary · no `threshold` vocabulary · no `data-zone`/`pane-collapse-toggle`-style consumer vocabulary | The start listener is attached **to the control element** (`§2.3` item 1); the tracking listeners are opened **only between start and terminal** (`§2.3` item 2); capture is **after establishment and per-control opt-in** (`§2.3` item 6); no selector/threshold/attribute vocabulary exists (`P-1`) | **`R-1`**, **`R-2`**, **`R-7`**, `M-1`, `M-14`, `I-4`, `I-7` |
| **P-8** | **No store and no persistence** (`SCH-2`'s acceptance; `H-r8` (C)#4) | **No file, no `localStorage`, no store object, no cache, no memo, no module-level mutable state**; the instance's own record is **one gesture deep, discarded at every terminal**, and only counters persist for the instance's lifetime (`§0A` note 8) | **`R-1`**, **`R-2`**, **`I-12`/`I-12b`**, `M-13`, **`P-GS-IM-6`**, `§7` item 7 |
| **P-9** | **No unverifiable criterion** (`H-r8` (C)#6): nothing whose falsification needs a layer this repo does not own, and **no geometry/magnitude claim** (the mandatory clause + `S-d9`'s *"origin REACHABILITY, NOT magnitude-equivalence"*) | **Every `§3` row is `[T]`**; **no row asserts a rendered-geometry, coordinate, layout, click-retargeting-in-a-browser or magnitude property**; **this spec offers no `[U]` row** and **claims no `[D]` row**; the module reads **no** pointer coordinate | **`R-8`**, **`I-11`**, `§5.2`, `§7` item 5, **`F-11`/`F-12`** |
| **P-10** | **No duplication of a sibling's responsibility** (`H-r6`'s dissolved-edge discipline; `A-d4`'s one-authority rule; validity `V-13`) | **No bounds/default/axis/threshold/drop/reveal/class/registry/census/projection responsibility appears here**; the module **imports no sibling** and **composes nothing** | **`R-4`**, **`R-5`**, `§1` items 5/6/8, **`F-8`** |

**The static rows `§2.2` cites are ENUMERATED, not asserted** (`§3.4` `R-1`..`R-9`): a prohibition citing *"a static source row"* with no id is **not** a row, and **every prohibition above names at least one id that exists in `§3.4`.**

### 2.3 The lifecycle rule — the session's whole contract, stated falsifiably

**Item 1 — ATTACHMENT: exactly one gesture-start listener per installed control, on ITS OWN element.**

| Order | Step | Exact behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **(a)** | `install(element, options?)` with a USABLE source and a distinct `element` | the session records the element **by identity** and calls **`source.on(element, POINTER_TYPES.start, handler)`** **exactly once**, where `handler` is bound to **that** element; returns `true` |
| **(b)** | the same `element` installed a second time | **NO source call at all**, and the return is `false`: **a repeat install is a NO-OP** ("a second `install` does not double listeners"); **the FIRST config wins and is not replaced** (`§2.4` item 2) |
| **(c)** | `install` with `null`/`undefined` element, a DISPOSED session, or an unusable source | **NO source call**, return `false`, and **no ledger entry is created** |
| **(d)** | the session's own `handler` runs (a real `pointerdown` on that control) | it calls **`begin(element)`**, and **the event object is not read and not passed anywhere** — the handler takes no parameter. **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-16` amendment — the THROWING-`onStart` CLEANUP CLAUSE, NEW text at this site; the as-filed cell above is kept visible): a `begin` whose `onStart` hook THROWS is NOT a SUCCESSFUL `begin`.** The `begin` attempt's order is the establishment checks (`§2.4` item 5), the three tracking attaches (item 2(a)), the capture call iff opted in (item 6(b)), then `onStart`; **a throw from `onStart` PROPAGATES to the caller of `begin`** (it is consumer code, and the session swallows only its OWN seam errors), and it leaves: **no listener of this attempt behind** (the three tracking listeners are detached on the failure path — item 2(c)'s detach), **the gesture counter NOT consumed** (`stats().gestures` does not increment), **the handle id NOT consumed** (the retry establishes with the id this attempt would have used, NOT the next one), **`gesture()` `null`**, and **the element still INSTALLED and re-establishable** (its install-time start listener is untouched, so a later `pointerdown` or an explicit `begin` establishes normally). **The clause is a RULING, not a measurement of the current bytes: the module's fix is the Implementer's and the TestWriter's row asserts the clause.** **The SUPERSEDED measured behaviour, kept visible because it was the reading a later pass could otherwise re-derive from the bytes: an `onStart` throw consumed the counter and the id (`stats().gestures === 1` with the retry receiving `id === 2`), which this clause REPLACES. Refused `begin`s consume nothing either (`ADV-GS-15`).** **⟶ CROSS-REFERENCE ADDED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-30` amendment): the establishment order this cell states is exactly the window in which item 7.7's MID-CALL DISPOSAL lands — a `dispose()` arriving from `capturePointer` (item 6(b), which runs BEFORE `onStart`) or from `onStart` itself is GOVERNED BY THE DISPOSAL, and the attempt then refuses `'disposed'` (item 7.7), so THIS cell's rule for a THROWING `onStart` and item 7.7's rule for a DISPOSING one are the two cases a reader deriving a row must not conflate.** |

**Item 2 — THE GESTURE WINDOW: tracking listeners exist ONLY between start and end.**

| Order | Step | Exact behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **(a)** | a successful `begin` | after the establishment checks (`§2.4` item 5), the session calls **`source.on(element, POINTER_TYPES.move, hMove)`**, **`source.on(element, POINTER_TYPES.end, hEnd)`**, **`source.on(element, POINTER_TYPES.cancel, hCancel)`** — **in that order, once each, on the SAME element**, so the session's listener count on the element goes from **`1`** to **`4`** |
| **(b)** | while the gesture is active | `hMove` runs the consumer's `onMove(gesture)`; `hEnd` runs the `end` terminal; `hCancel` runs the `cancel` terminal. **No listener is ever added to any OTHER element during a gesture** (no window/document tracking — `A-d3`'s rejected shape) |
| **(c)** | a terminal (`end`, `reset`, `cancel`) | the **FIRST** step is `source.off(element, POINTER_TYPES.move, hMove)` · `off(…, end, hEnd)` · `off(…, cancel, hCancel)` — **once each, same values, detach before any consumer code** (`§0A` note 11) — so the count returns to **`1`** and **the baseline is restored before `onEnd`/`onCancel`/`commit` run** |
| **(d)** | after the terminal | the element stays **installed** (its start listener remains) until `dispose()`; a new `pointerdown` establishes a **NEW** gesture with a **NEW** id |

**Item 3 — ONE GESTURE AT A TIME (the single-authority rule).** A `begin` while a gesture is active returns **`{ok:false, code:'busy'}`** and **changes nothing**: **no second gesture, no second listener set, no re-entrancy into the active gesture's value, no second commit**. A `pointerdown` on **another** installed control during an active gesture is likewise **`'busy'`** — the session is **one gesture authority for its whole instance**, which is the remedy `V-13` names: *"a second authority over the same gesture lifecycle"* is exactly what this item forbids.

**Item 4 — THE TERMINALS, with their commit counts (THE HEART OF THIS UNIT).**

| Terminal | Precondition | Detach | Hook | `commit` invocations | `outcome` | `TerminalResult` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **`end(element, gesture, value?)`** | `gesture` is the **active** handle (id AND element identity) | yes, all three, once each | `onEnd(element, final)` | **EXACTLY 1** | `'end'` | `{ok:true, code:'ok', committed:true}` |
| **`reset(element, gesture, value)`** | as above | yes, all three, once each | `onEnd(element, value)` | **EXACTLY 1**, of the **supplied** value | `'reset'` | `{ok:true, code:'ok', committed:true}` |
| **`cancel(element, gesture?)`** | `gesture` omitted, or the **active** handle | yes, all three, once each | `onCancel(element)` | **EXACTLY 0** | `'cancel'` | `{ok:true, code:'ok', committed:false}` |
| **any terminal on a stale/absent handle** | — | **NO detach** (nothing of this call's own), **NO hook**, **NO commit** | — | **0** | unchanged | `{ok:false, code:'stale' \| 'no-gesture', committed:false}` |
| **`dispose()` during an active gesture** | — | **ALL listeners**, start included | `onCancel(element)` | **EXACTLY 0** | `'cancel'` | `DisposeReport` |

**The final value rule, exact:** the value committed by `end` is **the `value` argument iff it is not `undefined`**, otherwise the gesture's current value (the last `gesture.set(…)` value), otherwise `undefined`. **The commit happens in EVERY successful `end`** — including an `end` whose value is `undefined` (`§0A` note 5) — so *"one commit per gesture"* is a **count**, never a *"commit only if something changed"* heuristic. **`reset` always commits its supplied value, which may be `undefined`** (the session supplies nothing).

**Item 5 — THE COMMIT IS THE SESSION'S CALL, AND IT IS COUNTED.** `commit` is invoked **by the session**, exactly once per gesture that reaches `end`/`reset`, **never** at `cancel`, **never** twice — and the session **counts its own invocations** (`stats().commits`, `gesture().commits`) so the count is assertable **without trusting the injected callback** (`§0A` note 12). **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-35` amendment — ANNOTATE-NEVER-REWRITE, so the as-filed clause above is kept visible): `gesture()`'s reading of that count is OBSERVABLE ONLY WHILE A GESTURE IS ACTIVE (where it is `0`), because a terminal DISCARDS the record and `gesture()` is then `null` (`§2.4` item 8, `M-17`) — so no clause, row or consumer may require `gesture().commits === 1` AFTER an `end`/`reset`. THE SESSION-SIDE READING OF THE COMMIT COUNT IS `stats().commits` (instance-lifetime, monotonic), read together with the injected `commit` sink's own call record and the terminal handle's `outcome`; this is the ruling already recorded at `§5.5.1 P-GS-SM-3` (`ADV-GS-22`(b)) and it is what the LANDED `M-4` row reads.** **The `committed` field of `TerminalResult` is that same record for the call at hand.**

**Item 6 — CAPTURE: ZERO CALLS BEFORE ESTABLISHMENT; AFTER IT, PER-CONTROL OPT-IN, AT MOST ONCE.**

| Case | `capture` value | Source calls for capture | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **(a)** | absent / `false` / any falsy (`0`, `''`, `null`, `NaN`, `undefined`) | **ZERO — the capture entry point is never called** | — |
| **(b)** | the consumer opts in (`true`, or any truthy value) | **exactly ONE**, on the gesture's OWN element | **inside `begin`, AFTER the tracking listeners are attached and AFTER the element is known connected** — i.e. **after establishment** |
| **(c)** | opt-in, but the gesture is later `cancel`led | the one capture call remains the gesture's **only** capture call; **no release call is invented** | — |

**⟶ PARKED — WITH ITS REVISIT CONDITION (`⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27`, the `ADV-GS-16` finding's ADJACENT OBSERVATION, recorded honestly rather than smoothed over; this is a `§2.3`-side note, NOT a `§5.5.1` register row and NOT a new clause).** **The observation, exactly as the finding names it: on the THROWING-`onStart` path (item 1(d)'s new clause) an OPT-IN control's capture call has ALREADY FIRED — the capture call precedes `onStart` in the establishment order (item 6(b)) — and the gesture then does NOT successfully begin. Item 6(c)'s rule applies to it: a release call is never invented, and NO clause of this contract requires the session to release.** **So the module does not release, this spec does not require a release, and the state is a RECORDED GAP IN THE CAPTURE LIFECYCLE rather than a contract breach — the parked follow-up (the supervisor files it in `docs/pending.md`) is whether a capture taken at a `begin` that then FAILS should be released by the session, which would be a NEW clause with its own gate (it would change the call counts item 6 pins, so it MAY NOT be landed by re-reading this item).** **Its revisit condition: a composing unit (`U-GUTTER` `E3` / `U-RELOCATE` `E4`) that needs the capture released on a failed establishment, or a real-renderer observation of a stuck capture, reopens it. No attempt term, strategy id, register row, code, census or section number moves for this note.**

**The rejected shape, named:** *"no capture at `pointerdown`"* is satisfied because **the capture call cannot happen from the start listener's own turn**: the start listener only calls `begin`, and the capture call inside `begin` is **after** the tracking listeners are open — i.e. **after the interaction is established** (`§0` ruling 2, `H-r9`, `Q2` = (a)). **A reading that forbade capture outright is explicitly NOT this contract** (it would make the fork's own `lostpointercapture` criterion inert and lose the pointer when the cursor leaves the window).

**Item 7 — DISPOSE: the baseline restore, exactly.**

1. **Cancel first, without committing:** an active gesture takes the `cancel` path (**`commit` zero times**, `onCancel` runs once).
2. **Detach everything the session attached:** the three tracking listeners of an in-flight gesture (if the cancel path has not already done so) **and every start listener of every installed control**, each with **exactly one `off(element, type, handler)`** carrying the same three values.
3. **Empty the ledger** and mark the instance **`disposed = true` FOREVER**: `install` returns `false` without a source call, `begin` reports `'disposed'`, every terminal reports `'disposed'` **without detaching or committing**, `stats()` reports `{installed: 0, active: false}`, `gesture()` returns `null`, and **the counters keep their last values** (they are not reset — `§0A` note 8). *(⟶ MOVED 2026-09-27, the `ADV-GS-30` amendment: the MID-CALL DISPOSAL clause first entered at THIS site was re-stated as item 7.7 at the END of this item 7's clause list, and its body appears ONCE — at `7.7` below; the disposal clauses 7.1–7.6 keep their own order and numbering, `7.7` follows `7.6`, and NO clause text was dropped by the move.)* **⟶ DELETED 2026-09-27 (the `ADV-GS-30` MOVE, `ADV-CN-6`/`D-06`'s precedent — delete the superseded/duplicated half, never keep both): the DUPLICATE COPY of the item 7.7 clause that stood at THIS site is REMOVED, because the clause body now appears verbatim at `7.7` and a second copy of a normative clause is exactly the `ADV-CN-6` class; the tombstone is kept so no reader mistakes the removal for a lost clause, and NO behaviour, code, count, term, id or section number changed.** **PROVENANCE, kept so the removal is attributable: the duplicated copy opened *"The measured behaviour this clause replaces, kept visible because it is the reading a later pass could otherwise re-derive from the pre-fix bytes: a `begin` whose session is disposed DURING the call…"* and the RULED text it carried — the disposal-governs ruling and the refusal clause — is verbatim at `7.7` below, which is the ONE site a reader takes it from.** **⟶ DELETED 2026-09-27 (the same MOVE, recorded at the site so nothing is left to inference): the `THE
