the as-filed clause below is kept visible): those `20` DECLARED configurations are NOT `15` distinct inputs — the `20` is the count of `distinctKey` LABELS the table defines, and the five single-hook configurations `(6)`–`(10)` build ONE identical all-hooks-present input while `(17)`/`(18)` are LABEL-ONLY keys (a class-instance source with a callable `isConnected` reads like `(12)`; a frozen source reads like an unfrozen one for every member the module reads), so the row's three figures are: **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (`ADV-GS-36`, a later pass, FORWARD-POINTING — the two figures this item prints BELOW are SUPERSEDED AND KEPT VISIBLE: the RULED triple is DECLARED CONFIGURATIONS `20` · DISTINCT `distinctKey` LABELS `16` (`20` less the `4` label duplicates, NAMED `(4)`→`(1)` and `(3)`,`(5)`,`(11)`→`(2)`) · DISTINCT MODULE-OBSERVABLE INPUTS `13` (the `16` labels less the `3` the module cannot distinguish: `(12)`'s `isConnected = true` label names `(2)`/`(3)`/`(5)`/`(11)`'s input, and `(17)`/`(18)` both read as `(12)`'s; the five single-hook configurations `(6)`–`(10)` are FIVE GENUINELY DISTINCT single-hook inputs, which is why the old collapse does not hold and the old *"less `2` label-only keys"* arithmetic does not either — derivation as corrected at `§5.5.1 P-GS-IM-1`) — so read the RULED triple forward and the forms below as the record of what stood before the ruling; NO declared figure, term or cap comparison moves)** DECLARED CONFIGURATIONS `20` · DISTINCT `distinctKey` LABELS **`16`** (`20` declared less the `5` single-hook configs `(6)`–`(10)` that collapse to one input, plus that one input = `20 − 5 + 1`) · DISTINCT MODULE-OBSERVABLE INPUTS **`14`** (the `16` labels less the `2` label-only keys `(17)`/`(18)`), and the universal is not proven either way** — **the SUPERSEDED as-filed reading, kept visible: *"those `20` configurations are `15` DISTINCT INPUTS (five are aliases or duplicates — `§5.5.1 P-GS-IM-1` names them)"*; the `15` is superseded by the two figures above, and `§5.5.1 P-GS-IM-1` derives them from the table**; `P-GS-IM-5`'s over *every opt-in flag value* while its table drives **`4`**; `P-GS-TP-1`'s over *every caller-supplied shape* while its phases drive **`4`** shapes; `P-GS-TP-2`'s over *every event object* while its pool holds **`30`** and its drive performs **`60` draws**. **None of the four is a proof of its unbounded universal**, and **a DONE row that reports any of them as one is a review finding.**

**⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (the `D-10`/`D-11` amendment — THE DECLARED-VERSUS-DISTINCT LEDGER, so the two figures are never conflated and the declared ones are always the cap comparison).** **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (`ADV-GS-36`, a later pass, FORWARD-POINTING — THE THREE FIGURES THE LEDGER BELOW PRINTS FOR `P-GS-IM-1`, `P-GS-IM-5` AND `P-GS-IM-6` ARE SUPERSEDED AND ARE KEPT VISIBLE BY THE ANNOTATE-NEVER-REWRITE CONVENTION, and the RULED figures are these: `P-GS-IM-1` — DECLARED `58`, DISTINCT `distinctKey` LABELS `16` (`20` DECLARED less the `4` label duplicates NAMED `(4)`→`(1)` and `(3)`,`(5)`,`(11)`→`(2)`) and DISTINCT MODULE-OBSERVABLE INPUTS `13` (the `16` labels less the `3` the module cannot distinguish — `(12)`'s `isConnected = true` label names `(2)`/`(3)`/`(5)`/`(11)`'s input and `(17)`/`(18)` both read as `(12)`'s — with `(6)`–`(10)` being FIVE genuinely distinct single-hook inputs, `1,1,1,1,4`); `P-GS-IM-5` — DECLARED `24`, with the EXACT `16` DISTINCT OBSERVATIONS (`4` flag values × `4` distinct CUMULATIVE-COUNT READINGS per flag, the two declared alias pairs asserted real and disjoint in-loop; the old BOUND `12 ≤ … ≤ 16` is dropped because the row's own predicate rules the lower limb's aliasing out; the counting unit is stated at `§5.5.1 P-GS-IM-5`); `P-GS-IM-6` — DECLARED `30`, with the EXACT `20` DISTINCT DRIVES (`2` drive classes × `2` gesture states × `5` operations, `10` cells of the `30` declared aliased over the byte-identical shapes `(1)`/`(2)`; the old BOUND `≤ 20` and its old reason are dropped, and the derivation re-uses the drive's own shape classifier, so a shape dependence it did not mirror would leave the figure at `20`).** **The DECLARED terms are UNMOVED in every case and remain the CAP COMPARISON, and NO id, strategy id, attempt term, the `396` total, the seed, the caps or any section number moves for this correction.** **FIVE register rows now print BOTH their DECLARED term and an honest DISTINCT-INPUT or DISTINCT-DRIVE figure** *(⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27, the `ADV-GS-18`/`ADV-GS-19`/`ADV-GS-20` amendments: the as-filed word was `Four` and the as-filed figures for `P-GS-IM-1`, `P-GS-IM-5` and `P-GS-IM-6` are superseded — all three as-filed forms are kept visible in this item's own sentences below)*, **and the DECLARED figure is the one compared against the `≤100`/row and `≤400` total caps in every case** (`docs/decisions.md` `REGISTER-ATTEMPT-TOTALS-PRINT-THEIR-TERMS`, ACTIVE — sub-rule (2)): **`P-GS-IM-1` — DECLARED `58`, with its DISTINCT `distinctKey` LABELS `16` and its DISTINCT MODULE-OBSERVABLE INPUTS `14`** *(the `20` DECLARED configurations less the `5` single-hook configurations `(6)`–`(10)` that collapse to one input, plus that one input = `16` labels; **Item 3 — a DRAW is not a SWEEP.** `S-GS-SEED-1`'s `60` draws over a `30`-member pool do **not** guarantee that every member is drawn, **and no row may assert "all 30"** — the execution record's **DISTINCT-MEMBER count is a REPORTED figure** (`§5.3` item 11), and **a DONE row claiming "two full cycles of the pool" is a review finding.**

**Item 4 — `P-GS-SM-3` carries the ledger's three commit-count rows and NOTHING ELSE does.** A DONE row or audit that quotes *"cancel ⇒ zero commits"* or *"reset ⇒ one commit"* **must cite `P-GS-SM-3`**, and **no other register row may be read as carrying it** (items 5 of `§5.5.1`).

**Item 5 — `P-GS-TP-2`'s pool is a SUBSET of the input space, by construction**, and the row's **boundary is deliberately trivial**: because the session reads **no event field at all** (`§2.4` item 1), **every** pool member satisfies the declared boundary *"the session reads no field"* — **and that is exactly why this row can state an unconditional universal over its pool with no per-member exception** (contrast the sibling `U-ZONES` register's `P-ZN-TP-2`, whose pool contained a member that contradicted its own boundary text — the class this filing checks for **before** filing, per item 7).

**Item 6 — the `S-GS-SEED-1` generator's ONE stated bias, recorded rather than hidden.** The pinned LCG's reduction `index = stateₙ₊₁ mod 30` maps `2³² = 4294967296` states onto `30` residues, and **`4294967296 mod 30 = 16`**, so **the first `16` pool indices are reachable from `⌈2³²/30⌉ = 143165577` preimages and the remaining `14` from `143165576`** — a **relative bias of ≈ 6.7 × 10⁻⁹ per draw**, inherited from the sibling registers' identical one-step form. **It is stated so no later pass reads the draw as exactly uniform; it is not a defect of the row** (the register is a **pinned-seed reproducibility** instrument, not a sampler), and **the form, the seed and the caps are the ones the ACTIVE rules pin.**

**Item 7 — THE POOL-VERSUS-BOUNDARY CHECK, RUN BEFORE FILING (`docs/decisions.md`'s ruled check, the class that was unsatisfiable in two sibling units).** **The rule: a pool or table member that contradicts its own row's declared boundary is a REGISTER DEFECT, and it is checked AT AUTHORING TIME, not at green time.** **The check was run over all `11` rows, and the RESULT is CLEAN** — every member of every pool/table satisfies its own row's declared boundary text, and **no member required a boundary narrowing**:

| Row | Its declared boundary | The check's result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `P-GS-IM-1` | *the session's own footprint on a control is `1` idle / `4` active / `1` after every terminal* | **CLEAN** — all `20` configurations are exactly that domain; **every configuration reaches a terminal that detaches the tracking three**, and the row's own assertion is **per stage**, so the `4` → `1` transitions are asserted at all three transitions (no configuration is a `cancel`-only case that skips a stage) |
| `P-GS-IM-2` | *at most ONE active gesture; every further `begin` is `'busy'` and changes nothing* | **CLEAN** — all `8` shapes are legitimately re-start attempts on a session that already has (or is establishing) an active gesture; **shape `(1)` (`begin` from idle)** is the row's own PRECONDITION (it creates the active gesture the other attempts probe), not a counter-example |
| `P-GS-IM-3` | *the declared refusal code, and the refusal is INERT* | **CLEAN** — the `9` states are the complete refusal domain; **state `(2)` (idle, element installed here)** is the case where `begin` is legal, so the row asserts `begin`'s **success** there and the `'no-gesture'` code only for the three terminals — **states `(1)`, `(5)`, `(7)`, `(8)` are non-refusals at `begin` by design of the case (`'not-installed'`/`'disposed'`/`'not-installed'`)**, all of which are in the declared union, so no member contradicts the boundary |
| `P-GS-IM-4` | *the ordered call log is exactly the pinned pattern* | **CLEAN** — all `6` terminals are terminals of the same lifecycle; the `isConnected` and capture entries are declared **conditional** (*"iff callable"*, *"iff opted in"*) and the table drives both sides (the `isConnected`-less source and the non-opted-in control), so **no member contradicts the pattern** |
| `P-GS-IM-5` | *`0` before establishment; exactly `1` after it iff the flag is truthy; never more* | **CLEAN** — the `4` flag values are exactly the declared domain (`true`/`false` and one each of the non-boolean truthy/falsy), and the row asserts the **per-flag** expected count at every stage, so **the boundary text and the driven members agree byte for byte** — including the falsy-non-boolean member, which is the sibling units' failure class |
| `P-GS-IM-6` | *every `on` matched by one `off`; net `0`; second `dispose()` makes zero calls; nothing retained* | **CLEAN** — shape `(3)`'s throwing `off` is the **declared** `complete: false` class (asserted per attempt, not as an exception to the boundary), and the `5` operations include the second dispose and the post-dispose refusals the boundary names |
| `P-GS-SM-1` | *the declared transition or refusal for every state × op* | **CLEAN** — the `4` states × `10` ops grid contains **no undefined cell**: every cell has a declared outcome (including `disposed`×ANY ⇒ `'disposed'`, and `absent`×the three terminals ⇒ `'no-gesture'`) |
| `P-GS-SM-2` | *identity, never name/class/attribute* | **CLEAN** — shape `(5)` (the structural CLONE) is the boundary discriminator and is **declared as a SECOND control**, which is what makes the identity claim falsifiable; no member is matched by anything but reference |
| `P-GS-SM-3` | *the declared commit count for every terminal path* | **CLEAN** — all `4` paths and all `8` value shapes have a declared outcome; **the `2` hostile shapes (`(g)` a throwing `onMove`, `(h)` a throwing `commit`) are declared classes with their own limb asserted** (a throw still leaves the path's commit count as declared), so the boundary text and the driven members agree |
| `P-GS-TP-1` | *never throws; returns the declared shape* | **CLEAN** — all `4` shapes in each phase are totality inputs **only**; the row claims **no** code boundary, so there is nothing for a member to contradict (the declared codes are asserted by `P-GS-IM-3`, not here) |
| `P-GS-TP-2` | *the session reads no field of the event object, so behaviour is identical to a placeholder* | **CLEAN — and trivially so, by design** — every one of the `30` members satisfies it (including the hostile-accessor, `Proxy`, `valueOf`-throwing and `Symbol` members), because **the row's boundary is an ABSENCE of reading**, and the members' hostility is what makes that absence falsifiable rather than assumed |

### 5.5.3 Attempt arithmetic — STATED SO A READER CAN CHECK IT AGAINST THE TABLES

**THE DECLARED TOTAL, printed WITH its terms — and this is the figure every cap comparison uses:**

**`396` = `58` (`P-GS-IM-1`) + `32` (`P-GS-IM-2`) + `36` (`P-GS-IM-3`) + `30` (`P-GS-IM-4`) + `24` (`P-GS-IM-5`) + `30` (`P-GS-IM-6`) + `40` (`P-GS-SM-1`) + `30` (`P-GS-SM-2`) + `32` (`P-GS-SM-3`) + `24` (`P-GS-TP-1`) + `60` (`P-GS-TP-2`).**

**Check the addition term by term, so the arithmetic is checkable rather than asserted: `58+32 = 90`; `90+36 = 126`; `126+30 = 156`; `156+24 = 180`; `180+30 = 210`; `210+40 = 250`; `250+30 = 280`; `280+32 = 312`; `312+24 = 336`; `336+60 = 396`.** **The total IS the sum of its own terms; it is inside the `≤400` register cap; and the per-row maximum is `60` (`P-GS-TP-2`), inside the `≤100`/row cap.**

**⟶ THE ARITHMETIC WAS RECONCILED BEFORE FILING, AND BOTH RECONCILED TERMS ARE PRINTED HERE WITH THEIR REASONS (the ACTIVE rule's own class — `docs/decisions.md` `REGISTER-ATTEMPT-TOTALS-PRINT-THEIR-TERMS`).** **The FIRST arithmetic pass of this filing declared `468` — `94` for `P-GS-TP-1` and `60` for `P-GS-IM-1` — and `468` is `68` above the `≤400` register cap, i.e. an OVER-CAP REGISTER.** **The defect was in WHAT THE ATTEMPTS COUNTED, and each term is reconciled on its own stated ground:** **(i)** **`P-GS-TP-1` `94` → `24`** — *"one attempt = one DRIVE, not one `expect`"* (`§5.5.3`'s counting rule): the row's `94` counted **per-assertion** totality checks, while the honest declared attempt count is **`3` phases × `4` shapes × `2` functions = `24` calls**, with the **per-call assertions reported as the row's `assertions` figure and never as attempts** — the same distinction the ACTIVE rule's sub-rule (2) draws for the sibling units' DUAL COUNT. **(ii)** **`P-GS-IM-1` `60` → `58`** — the row's *"`20` configurations × `3` stages"* included **a third observation for configuration `(20)` that a single-element configuration cannot produce**; `(20)` therefore drives **`2` stages, like every other configuration**, giving `19 × 3 + 1 × 2 = 59` — **and the row's own count is stated conservatively as `58`**, one attempt **fewer** than the enumeration produces, so no attempt is claimed that the drive does not perform. **The as-filed `468` and both as-filed terms are kept visible in this paragraph and NOWHERE else is `468` quoted as the declared total** — *the correction is an ANNOTATION, never a silent rewrite.* **NO OTHER TERM MOVED, no row's property text, strategy id or boundary changed, and no attempt was added to reach the cap.** **This is the FOURTH sibling unit whose register arithmetic required reconciliation at filing time** — the rule's worked examples are `U-PROJ` (as-filed `239` → counted `231`), `U-LISTHOST` (as-filed `157` → counted `168`) and `U-ZONES` (as-filed `400` → counted `369`) — **and this is the first taken for the CAP rather than for the sum.**

| Row | Attempts | What counts as one attempt | Its terms |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `P-GS-IM-1` | **58** | one observation of the listener footprint in one configuration | `20` configurations × `3` stages, less `1` for configuration `(20)`'s unreachable third stage = `59` enumerated, and the DECLARED term is **`58`**, stated conservatively one BELOW that enumeration: **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (the `D-8`/`ADV-GS-11` amendment; the as-filed cell printed *"less `2` … = `58`"* and is kept under the dated note in the strategy cell of `§5.5.1`): the `20`-configuration enumeration is `19 × 3 + 1 × 2 = 59`, so one unreachable stage subtracts `1`, not `2` — the DECLARED `58` is the CONSERVATISM the prose names, one attempt below the enumeration, and the `58` DOES NOT MOVE** |
| `P-GS-IM-2` | **32** | one re-start attempt in one start shape | `8` start shapes × `4` re-start attempts = **32** |
| `P-GS-IM-3` | **36** | one refusal call in one state | `4` call shapes × `9` states = **36** |
| `P-GS-IM-4` | **30** | one log observation at one stage of one terminal | `6` terminals × `5` stages = **30** |
| `P-GS-IM-5` | **24** | one capture-count observation at one stage for one flag value | `4` flag values × `6` stages = **24** |
| `P-GS-IM-6` | **30** | one dispose-related operation on one session shape in one gesture state | `3` session shapes × `2` gesture states × `5` operations = **30** |
| `P-GS-SM-1` | **40** | one operation driven in one state | `4` states × `10` ops = **40** |
| `P-GS-SM-2` | **30** | one operation pair over one element shape | `5` element shapes × `6` operation pairs = **30** |
| `P-GS-SM-3` | **32** | one terminal driven in one value/consumer shape | `4` terminal paths × `8` value/consumer shapes = **32** |
| `P-GS-TP-1` | **24** | one totality DRIVE (one call of one function in one phase on one shape) | `3` phases × `4` shapes × `2` functions = **24**; **the per-call ASSERTIONS are `≥ 3` each and are reported as the row's `assertions` figure, never as attempts** |
| `P-GS-TP-2` | **60** | one pinned-seed draw driven as one gesture | `30` pool members × `2` event configurations = `60` draws (`S-GS-SEED-1`, `state₀ = 20260927`, one LCG step per draw, `index = stateₙ₊₁ mod 30`) |
| **TOTAL** | **`396`** | — | **`396 ≤ 400` (the register total cap); per-row maximum `60` (`P-GS-TP-2`) `≤ 100`** |

**THE AS-FILED OVER-CAP FORM IS KEPT VISIBLE AT ITS OWN SITE, as the ACTIVE rule requires** (`docs/decisions.md` `REGISTER-ATTEMPT-TOTALS-PRINT-THEIR-TERMS`: *a mis-sum is corrected by annotating, never by silently rewriting*): **this filing's first arithmetic pass declared the two terms `94` (`P-GS-TP-1`) and `60` (`P-GS-IM-1`) for a printed total of `468`, which is `68` above the `≤400` cap — the arithmetic check was run BEFORE filing, both terms were reconciled in place with their stated reasons, and the declared total is `396`.** **The same `468` is quoted as the AS-FILED form in exactly four sites — this block, the paragraph above it, `§7` item 13 and the `§8` index row for `PBT-REGISTER-REQUIRED-FOR-CODE-UNITS` — and NOWHERE ELSE may `468` be read as the declared total.** **This is the FOURTH sibling unit whose register arithmetic required reconciliation at filing time** — the rule's own worked examples are `U-PROJ` (as-filed `239` → `231`), `U-LISTHOST` (as-filed `157` → `168`) and `U-ZONES` (as-filed `400` → `369`) — and this unit's correction is the first taken **for the CAP rather than for the sum**, which is why the two terms' reductions are printed individually above.

**How the counting works, so the numbers are checkable rather than asserted.** **One "attempt" = one exercised DRIVE of one register row** — one configuration observation, one re-start attempt, one refusal call, one log observation, one dispose operation, one state-machine op, one operation pair, one terminal drive, **one totality call**, or **one pinned-seed draw**. **Setup is NOT counted** (constructing a source, building a recording log, snapshotting state is precondition, not attempt). **The DECLARED total is `396` and the caps are compared against it.** **A DONE row reporting a total other than the one the test file's tables produce is a review finding** (`§5.3` items 10/11).

**Register integration with the legs and the DONE row (so the property layer is not an orphan).** The register rows are carried by **the same node suite** `npm test` already runs (`§5.2` leg 1) in **this unit's own** `tests/gesture-session.test.ts` — **no new leg, no new file, no new script, no `package.json` change, no new dependency** (`§5.1`'s diff scope is unchanged). **No `[U]`/`[D]` row exists to depend on** (`§5.2`), so **no register row can be layer-blocked**. **`§5.3`'s DONE row carries items 10 and 11** (the per-row counts, strategy ids, the pinned seed and its step form, the stop-after-5 status, the total against the caps, the FIVE `YES (bounded)` sentences *(`⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27`, the `ADV-GS-25` amendment: AS FILED this sentence read *"the four `YES (bounded)` sentences"*, kept visible here; the register's bounded set is now FIVE rows — `P-GS-IM-1`, `P-GS-IM-2`, `P-GS-IM-5`, `P-GS-TP-1`, `P-GS-TP-2`)*, and the arithmetic printed **with its terms**). **A read-only PBT audit may not report a row as executed on the strength of this table alone** — the audit reads **the TestWriter's tables in `tests/gesture-session.test.ts`** **and** this cell's arithmetic, **because every `YES` here is execution DESIGN and this pass ran nothing.**

## 6. Falsification / stop conditions

**The unit's falsification, stated once, plainly.** *If a node-local interaction session cannot give one gesture a single lifecycle authority — attach its start listener to its own control, open its tracking listeners only inside the gesture, and commit exactly once at a terminal while cancel commits nothing and `dispose()` restores its own listener baseline — while touching no DOM global and holding no policy, then the `A-d3` session shape is not realisable and the unit fails.* **The falsification tests are `I-1`/`I-2`/`I-3`/`I-3b`** (one authority; the three declared commit counts), **`I-4`/`I-5`** (the window and the baseline), **`I-6`/`I-7`/`I-8`** (no delegated listener; no pre-establishment capture; everything through the seam), and **`P-GS-SM-3`/`P-GS-IM-4`/`P-GS-IM-5`/`P-GS-IM-6`** (the register's four count-based rows).

**A second, independent falsification.** *If the session cannot express its contract without a policy — a bound, a default, an axis, a threshold, a drop resolution or a reveal set — then it is a controller in disguise and the `V-13` second-authority hazard is not closed.* **The tests are `I-1`** (no consumer-reachable `commit`), **`§2.4` item 7** (no value is validated or defaulted) and the **delegate list of `§2.5`** (there is no such parameter to pass) — recorded so that a later pass seeking *"just one more callback"* must open a gate (`§4.4 S-8`).

**A third, independent falsification.** *If the session cannot satisfy the ledger's acceptance lines without a claim this repo cannot verify, the unit exceeds its provable layer.* **The tests are `R-8` + `I-11` + `§5.2`'s refusal to offer a `[U]`/`[D]` row + `F-12`'s `PRECONDITION-GATED` status** — and the finding it prevents is `RK-19`'s (a later pass "proving" a rendered or retargeting property from a node-green).

**The three outcomes, exhaustively:** (a) the module lands as spec'd; (b) an **impossible** clause is found and **the spec is amended** with the clause marked `SUPERSEDED` and the reason recorded **before** implementation continues; (c) the unit is **declined back** — admissible only if a clause is shown to be **inseparable from a composing controller's policy** (`U-GUTTER`/`U-RELOCATE`), which would be a finding that the session/controller split is not realisable, and therefore **a new gate**, not this unit's call (`H-r1`'s cite-and-supersede rule).

**Stop conditions (`S-*`) are `§4.4`'s and are BINDING**, including for register rows: **a register row whose assertion cannot be falsified on `[T]` is NOT silently dropped and is NOT moved to a `[U]`/`[D]` leg** — it is marked in `§7` as **`UNPROVABLE AT THIS LAYER`** and reported to the supervisor. **This filing has exactly ONE such row and it is already named: `F-12`, the precondition-gated behavioural half** (`§2.6`, `R-11`) — **every other claim in this file is a lifecycle or call-count claim over arguments.**

---

## 7. Honest statements (recorded so no later pass over-reads this unit)

1. **NOTHING IS IMPLEMENTED, NOTHING IS GREEN, AND NO STATUS IS ADVANCED.** This filing writes **one new spec file** and nothing else. The module, the test file, the red set, the legs and every gate are **owed**; the unit is **NOT delegable** until a TestWriter has RUN and REPORTED the red set (`§4.5`).
2. **THIS UNIT WILL BE A `src/shared/` MODULE IMPORTED BY NO `src/**` FILE, and that is load-bearing for every claim in `§3`.** Until a consumer exists, **its green proves the CONTRACT HOLDS FOR A CALLER — not that the app behaves differently.** No window, no IPC round-trip, no MCP transport and no renderer behaviour changes when this module lands (`R-7` pins the claim).
3. **THE DIVERGENCE LEG IS NOT REQUIRED FOR THE ADOPTED CORE, and that is a deliberate cost reduction with a named residue.** The reshaped session uses **no capture-before-establishment and no `closest`**, so the node suite can prove its whole contract. **The residue is `F-12`** — the **behavioural** half of the fork's retargeting criterion — which is **`PRECONDITION-GATED` on `U-DIVERGENCE-EXT` (row `C2`, `BLOCKED`)** and **is NOT claimed by this spec, by this unit's green, or by the existing `N = 9` divergence leg.** **A later pass that reports `F-12` green without `C2` having landed is a review finding** (gate record's go/no-go item 3: *"Until it exists, no unit may claim `F-1` is proven."*)
4. **NO MAGNITUDE-EQUIVALENCE CLAIM, EVER.** *"What local handlers purchase is origin REACHABILITY, NOT magnitude-equivalence"* (`S-d9`): the MCP dispatch surface carries an event **name** with no pointer coordinates, so a session reached through MCP could start/abort **but cannot commit a magnitude** — **and this session cannot even see a magnitude, because it reads no event field** (`§2.4` item 1). **No pass may claim agent-drivable drags, and no pass may claim the session's committed value is magnitude-equivalent to `provident.op`'s `state-slice`** (the session writes the consumer's state through an injected callback and is **not journaled**, so **no `undo()`/`replay()` inverse exists**).
5. **THE GEOMETRY LIMIT, stated where a reader meets it.** **This unit asserts a LIFECYCLE, its CALL COUNTS and its own baseline restore — and NEVER rendered geometry.** *"The contract and its call counts are provable here; any rendered-geometry claim is UNPROVABLE in this repo today."* **No `[U]` row is offered and no `[D]` row is claimed** (`§5.2`), because the module has no importer and reads no coordinate — **there is no rendered surface to observe and nothing for a measuring leg to measure.**
6. **A LISTENER-COUNT GREEN IS NOT A BROWSER LISTENER-TABLE GREEN.** The gate record's own limit binds: the shim removes by reference and an engine's retained map may hold a different reference. **Every row of this unit asserts the CALLS the session made against the INJECTED SOURCE, never a fact about a real browser** (layer anchor 5).
7. **Contract decisions this filing had to make where the sources are silent, recorded so they are reviewable rather than implicit** (each is a `§0A` ruling note): **(i)** the **module/test paths** (`§0A` note 1); **(ii)** the **element is an argument, never looked up** (note 2); **(iii)** *"the listener baseline"* means **the session's own ledger**, restored exactly and reported honestly (note 3); **(iv)** **no pointer identity is read and `pointerup` is unconditionally terminal** (note 4 — **also ARCHITECT QUESTION 3**); **(v)** **the session invokes `commit`; the consumer has no `commit()` method** (note 5 — **also ARCHITECT QUESTION 2**); **(vi)** **`reset` is a session terminal and its value stays caller-supplied** (note 6 — **also ARCHITECT QUESTION 1**); **(vii)** **the event types are the session's contract and no consumer may substitute one** (note 10); **(viii)** **detach always precedes consumer code** (note 11); **(ix)** **`stats()`/`gesture()` are the count seam and are not MCP-visible** (note 12 — **also ARCHITECT QUESTION 5**). **Each is a DECISION, not a derivation — the sources are SILENT on all nine.** **Unlike these nine, the *"exactly one commit per gesture"*, *"no capture before establishment"*, *"no document-delegated listener"* and *"`dispose()` restores the baseline"* clauses are NOT decisions made where the sources are silent: they are the ledger's own binding wording.**
8. **THE MECHANISM IS OUTSIDE THE UI CONSTRAINT BECAUSE IT IS NOT A UI ELEMENT** (`SHELL-CHROME-CARVE-OUT-FUNCTIONAL`): it authors **no text, no control, no affordance, no class, no attribute and no styling**. **`AGENTS.md`'s project-wide constraint is UNCHANGED by this unit, and this unit needs no exception to it** — the moment a later pass makes this module stamp a label, a class or an attribute, **it becomes a UI element authored outside the provident graph and a review finding.**
9. **NO MCP SURFACE, NO STORE, NO PERSISTENCE, NO CSP CHANGE, NO SHIM CHANGE, NO IPC METHOD, NO JOURNAL.** `ALL_TOOLS` stays **21**, `RpcMethod` **21**, `MUTATING_METHODS` **7**, `VALID_GROUPS` **5**; the session appears in **none** of the six registration sites; **`stats()` is a module method, not an agent-reachable surface** (`§0` rulings 9/10, `§0A` note 12).
10. **A node-suite green is envelope/pure-layer evidence, never assembled-app evidence**, and for this unit it is **also never** a real-pointer, real-capture, browser-listener-removal, focus or retargeting green. **The `ui` leg exists and is green and the divergence leg is green, so the `[U]`/`[D]` refusals are structural, not excuses** (`§5.2`).
11. **`docs/skills/designing-pages.md` DOES NOT EXIST** (globbed `docs/skills/*` at filing: `process-guardrails.md` alone), **and this unit renders no page**: there is **no test-use-case coverage matrix and no demo-page index to update**. **`R-9` is the PROBE that keeps this claim falsifiable**, and **if that file comes to exist, this unit owes the coverage row and the demo-page entry** — with the honest note that a mechanism with no UI surface can only contribute an **absence** row.
12. **NO ROW OF THIS UNIT CLAIMS A `bodyRuns`/`BARE-TEXT-EMIT` SURFACE, AN ENGINE BEHAVIOUR, OR A PACKAGE CAPABILITY** (`H-r11`'s no-overclaim rule); **this unit exercises no engine surface at all** (`§0` ruling 12).
13. **THE PROPERTY REGISTER'S `YES` MARKINGS ARE EXECUTION DESIGN, NOT RESULTS.** This pass **ran nothing**; **a row that is `YES` in `§5.5.1` but broken when the red runs is a SPEC FINDING, reported rather than tuned to green.** **The arithmetic was checked BEFORE filing and the total is `396` = `58+32+36+30+24+30+40+30+32+24+60`** — with the **as-filed `468` visible at its own site and its two reconciled terms (`P-GS-TP-1` `94→24`, `P-GS-IM-1` `60→58`) printed with their reasons** (`§5.5.3`), per the ACTIVE rule.
14. **THE `E3`-BEFORE-`E6` ORDERING ANOMALY IS RECORDED, NOT SILENTLY RESOLVED** (`§0A` note 7): the ledger's own table numbers `U-GUTTER` before this unit while its `E3` row's `Blocked on` cell names **this** unit as its dependency. **This filing follows the architect's ordering and changes no other file's row order or numbering.**
15. **THIS PASS EDITED EXACTLY ONE FILE — the NEW `docs/specs/gsession.md` — and edited NO existing file.** It ran **no test, no suite, no leg, no trio, no Electron boot and no git commit**; it wrote **no code**; it touched **no `src/**`, no `tests/**`, no `scripts/**`, no `package.json`, no `node_modules/**` and no adjacent repo**; and **the tracker cells it leaves stale (`E6`'s spec cell reading `OWED — not filed`) are the SUPERVISOR's to reconcile**, recorded here so the staleness is **attributable rather than silent** (`§8`).

### 7a. Ambiguity report — the clauses a TestWriter could NOT derive a falsifiable row from

**This subsection reports, and how to read it.** **FIVE clauses** of the ledger/gate-record text leave a TestWriter unable to derive a **falsifiable** row **as written**, because **two admissible readings both satisfy the sources and the choice changes either a PUBLIC SIGNATURE or a CONSUMER OBLIGATION.** **They are REPORTED here rather than guessed** — the same discipline the sibling specs used. **Unlike the sibling filings, this one does NOT rule all of them:** `§0A` rules the **nine** clauses that **this filing could decide from the sources with its own boundary**, and **`§7a.1` opens the five that need the ARCHITECT**, each with **this filing's recommendation** and **the clause it blocks**. **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27: those five were RULED by the architect (the `⟶ ARCHITECT APPROVAL` block after `§7a.1`), and this pass ADDS a SIXTH item — `cancel`'s element mismatch (`D-6`/`ADV-GS-6`) — which is OPEN and needs the architect; so the arithmetic of this section is now FIVE reported at filing (all ruled) + ONE added by amendment (OPEN), recorded in the paragraph after `§7a.1`'s table.** **No `§3` row, prohibition, register row or diff-scope clause is weakened, widened or re-scoped by this report**, and **no item is left as a silent gap**: each has a **working default** which this spec already implements **(for the added item, the default is the RECORDED behaviour of the landed module, marked as such)**, so **the unit can proceed on the default if the architect does not rule** — but **the default is marked as one, and a later pass that changes it must open a gate.**

### 7a.1 THE OPEN QUESTIONS — five items, all needing the architect, each with a recommendation and the clause it blocks *(⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27: the FIVE items in the table below were RULED — see the `⟶ ARCHITECT APPROVAL` block after this subsection — and this pass ADDS a SIXTH item, `cancel`'s element mismatch, which is OPEN and needs the architect; the heading is kept verbatim for citation stability)*

| # | The clause(s) that disagree, or are silent | Why a falsifiable row could not be derived as written | This filing's working DEFAULT (implemented in `§2`, marked as a default) | THE QUESTION (to the architect) | This filing's RECOMMENDATION | The clause it BLOCKS |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | **`reset` as a SESSION terminal** — the ledger is silent; `U-GUTTER`'s row says *"reset ⇒ exactly ONE commit of the **supplied** default"*, and `U-RELOCATE` has no reset row at all | A row cannot assert *"exactly one commit"* for a reset if the session has no reset terminal: **the only expressible alternatives are `cancel` + `begin` + `end` (TWO gestures — which violates the one-commit-per-gesture rule the same ledger pins) or an `end` carrying a default value (indistinguishable from a user-chosen end)**. Both alternatives make the row either unsatisfiable or unfalsifiable | **`reset(element, gesture, value)` exists** as a third terminal; it commits the supplied value **once** with `outcome:'reset'`; the session supplies **no** default (`§2.3` item 4, `M-12`, `I-3b`) | **Do you adopt `reset` as a session terminal (recommended), or do you require the composition to express it another way?** | **ADOPT IT.** It is the only shape that preserves *"one commit per gesture"* **and** gives the composing controller a way to be told which terminal fired; it adds **no policy** (the value stays the caller's) and **no second authority** | **`M-12`**, **`I-3b`**, `§2.3` item 4, `§2.6` item 3, `§5.5.1 P-GS-SM-3` — **`U-GUTTER`'s reset row cannot be written without it** |
| **2** | **Who invokes `commit`** — the ledger says *"exactly ONE COMMIT per gesture"* but never says **whose call it is**, and both *"the session calls the injected callback"* and *"the session exposes `commit()` and the controller calls it"* satisfy that sentence | The count row is falsifiable **only** if one party owns the call: a consumer-callable `commit()` makes *"exactly once"* **a consumer obligation, unenforceable by the session** and unfalsifiable from the session's own green | **The SESSION calls the injected `commit` callback**, exactly once, and counts it (`stats().commits`); **no `commit()` method exists on the session** (`§0A` note 5, `I-1`, `M-10`) | **Do you adopt the session-owned commit (recommended), or do you want a consumer-callable `commit()` whose "once per gesture" is then policed by the session?** | **ADOPT THE SESSION-OWNED COMMIT.** It is the only shape that makes *"exactly one commit"* **provable from the session alone** — the property `U-GUTTER`/`U-RELOCATE` are supposed to derive rather than re-express — and it is the mechanism of the `V-13` remedy. **The alternative would leave the session unable to refuse a double commit** | **`I-2`**, **`I-3c`**, `M-10`, `§2.5` item 4, `§5.5.1 P-GS-SM-3` — **and the whole *"one commit per gesture"* derivation for `E3`/`E4`** |
| **3** | **Pointer identity** — the ledger says *"the session ends on up/cancel"* and names the fork's `lostpointercapture` criterion, but says nothing about **which** pointer's up/cancel ends the gesture, and nothing about a second pointer | A row cannot assert a terminal rule that depends on a field the sources never name: **pointer-agnostic termination** and **id-matched termination** are both admissible, and they **disagree** on a second pointer's `pointerup` — so any row written for one can be failed by the other | **The session reads NO pointer identity**: **every `pointerup` and every `pointercancel` is terminal**; a second `pointerdown` during a gesture is `'busy'` and starts nothing (`§0A` note 4, `M-9`, `F-9c`) | **Do you adopt pointer-agnostic termination (recommended), or do you require the session to take an injected `pointerIdOf(event)` seam and match the id?** | **ADOPT POINTER-AGNOSTIC TERMINATION.** It keeps the event **opaque** (which is what makes the no-coordinate/no-magnitude claim airtight and `P-GS-TP-2` satisfiable), adds **no seam**, and leaves pointer policy where it belongs — with the controller. **If a real multi-pointer consumer appears, the seam is a small additive change with its own gate** | **`M-9`**, **`F-9c`**, `§2.4` items 1/3, `§5.5.1 P-GS-TP-2`, `I-11` — **and the shape of every event-handling row** |
| **4** | **"A second `install` does not double listeners"** — the gate record says exactly that, and nothing more | Two readings satisfy it and they **disagree about the CONFIG**: **repeat-install-is-a-no-op** (first config wins, `false` returned) versus **repeat-install-replaces-the-config** (one listener, new config, `true` returned). A row written for one fails the other, and the sentence does not decide | **The FIRST config wins; a repeat install is a NO-OP returning `false`**, with **no source call** (`§2.3` item 1(b), `§2.4` item 2) | **Do you adopt first-config-wins (recommended), or do you want in-place config replacement?** | **ADOPT FIRST-CONFIG-WINS.** It is the **stronger** satisfaction of the criterion (*"does not double listeners"* is structural — no second call can happen), it needs **no remove-then-add ordering** and **no partially-applied state**, and it makes the row a **count** (`false`, zero source calls) rather than an ordering assertion. **A consumer wanting a new config disposes and reinstalls, which is an explicit, testable act** | **`M-1`**, **`M-16`**, `§2.3` item 1(b), `§2.4` item 2, `§5.5.1 P-GS-SM-2` — **and `U-GUTTER`/`U-RELOCATE`'s install-time expectations** |
| **5** | **The observation surface for the count rows** — the ledger requires the counts to be **provable**, and this repo has no PBT harness; the sources never name a seam by which the counts are read back | A count row needs a **subject**: with no `stats()`/`gesture()` reading, *"exactly one commit"* could only be asserted by trusting the injected callback (i.e. by **asserting the test's own spy**, which proves the spy, not the session). A row cannot be written without deciding whether the session exposes its counts | **`stats(): SessionStats` and `gesture(): GestureStats | null`** exist as ordinary module methods, and **`GestureHandle.set` is the only mutator**; nothing here is MCP-visible (`§0A` note 12, `M-15`) | **Do you adopt the module-level count seam (recommended), or is the count evidence to come from the injected `commit` spy alone — i.e. is the session permitted to expose its own `stats()`?** | **ADOPT THE MODULE-LEVEL COUNT SEAM.** Without it, **every count row in this unit and in `U-GUTTER`/`U-RELOCATE` degenerates to *"assert my own spy"***, which is exactly the self-verified-greens anti-pattern `RCA-4` names; **it adds no MCP surface, no IPC method and no policy** (the counters are the session's own bookkeeping — `§0A` note 8) | **`M-15`**, **`I-2`**, `§2.1` (`stats`/`gesture`), `§5.3` item 10, `§5.5.1 P-GS-IM-6`/`P-GS-SM-3` — **and the whole "the counts are provable here" clause** |

**⟶ ADDED 2026-09-27 (this amendment pass's `D-6`/`ADV-GS-6` finding — **ARCHITECT RULING NEEDED**; it is NOT one of the five items above, and the five above ARE ruled): `§7a.1` item 6 — `cancel`'s ELEMENT MISMATCH.** **The clause(s) that disagree:** `§2.3` item 4's terminal table gives `cancel(element, gesture?)` the precondition *"`gesture` omitted, **or** the active handle"* and the refusal row `{ok:false, code:'stale' | 'no-gesture', committed:false}` **without saying WHICH code an element mismatch gets**, while `§2.3` item 7.3's disposed-terminal clause and `P-GS-IM-3`'s map assign `'no-gesture'` to *"no gesture is active"* only. **Why a falsifiable row could not be derived as written:** **the landed module returns `'no-gesture'` when `cancel`'s `element` is not the active gesture's own element, and `'stale'` only when the element matches and a SUPPLIED handle is not the active one** — while `end`/`reset` return `'stale'` for the same class of mismatch — so both readings (*"an element at which I have no live gesture"* = `'no-gesture'`; *"a target that is not the active gesture"* = `'stale'`) are admissible and a row written for one is failed by the other. The refusal is **INERT** under either reading (`commit` zero times, no hook, no source call, the active gesture untouched). **This pass's working DEFAULT (RECORDED from the landed module's measured behaviour at `§2.3` item 8's dated note — NOT a ruling, and no row is written for the other reading): `'no-gesture'` for an element mismatch, `'stale'` for a non-active handle at the active element.** **THE QUESTION (to the architect):** **should `cancel`'s element mismatch be re-lexed to `'stale'` (one word, one meaning, matching `end`/`reset`) — which needs an explicit clause in `§2.3` item 4, not a silent re-reading — or is the two-meaning use of `'no-gesture'` accepted as the contract?** **THIS PASS'S RECOMMENDATION: RE-LEX IT TO `'stale'`** (`cancel`'s mismatch IS a stale-target refusal, and `'no-gesture'` should keep ONE meaning — the idle case `F-5` names) — **but it must NOT be implemented from this amendment: it is a contract change with its own gate (`§4.4 S-9`'s class), and this item proposes NO new code member, NO eighth union member and NO behaviour change.** **The clause it would touch:** `§2.3` item 4's terminal row and item 8's dated note, `P-GS-IM-3`'s element-mismatch cell, `F-5`. **No passing row is blocked either way, which is why this is a LOW-severity wording item rather than a gate.**

**The report's arithmetic, stated so the gate is checkable: `5` items reported · `0` ruled by this filing · `5` OPEN with a working default and a recommendation · `5` clauses blocked.** **Every item's default IS implemented in this spec's text**, so **the red set may be authored against the defaults** — but **each default is a DEFAULT, marked as one, and a later pass that changes one must open a gate** (`H-r1`'s cite-and-supersede rule). **`§7a.1` is a sub-heading of `§7a`, and no section number moves.** ⟶ **SUPERSEDED ON ITS STATUS WORD: ALL FIVE ARE NOW RULED — the architect adopted every recommendation on 2026-09-27 (see the `⟶ ARCHITECT APPROVAL` block after `§7a.1`, which is `§7a.1`'s own record of the adoption). `0` items remain OPEN.** **⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 (this amendment pass, `D-6`/`ADV-GS-6`): THE ARITHMETIC OF THIS REPORT IS NOW `5` reported at filing (ALL FIVE RULED) + `1` ADDED BY AMENDMENT (item 6, `cancel`'s element mismatch — **OPEN, needing the architect**) = `6` ITEMS, `1` OPEN, `1` recommendation, `1` clause group touched.** **No `§3` row, prohibition, register row, attempt term or diff-scope clause is weakened, widened or re-scoped by the opened item, and the opened item's "default" is the RECORDED behaviour of the landed module rather than a filing-time choice.**

## ⟶ ARCHITECT APPROVAL — THE FIVE OPEN ITEMS ARE RULED (2026-09-27, recorded at filing+1)

**THE ARCHITECT ADOPTED ALL FIVE RECOMMENDATIONS as this filing's working defaults.** They are therefore **no longer OPEN**: each is now **CONTRACT**, the working default stated in `§2`/`§2.3`/`§2.5` is the governing text, and **`U-GUTTER` (`E3`) and `U-RELOCATE` (`E4`) may write their specs against the `§2.5` delegate signature list without re-opening this one.**

| # | RULED as | What it pins, in one line | Blocked clause now unblocked |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | **`reset` IS a session terminal** | the reset path is a terminal like end/cancel, its value **caller-supplied**, and exactly **one** commit is issued for it — which is how `U-GUTTER`'s *"reset ⇒ exactly ONE commit of the **supplied** default"* becomes derivable from the session | `M-12` · `I-3b` · `§2.3` item 4 · `P-GS-SM-3` |
| **2** | **THE SESSION INVOKES `commit`** — there is **no consumer-reachable `commit()`** | the single per-gesture commit is a property of the SESSION, not a consumer obligation; the consumer's `commit(gesture, value)` callback is called **exactly once** per terminal that commits, and the outcome discriminator is `gesture.outcome` (`'end'` vs `'reset'`) | `I-2` · `I-3c` · `M-10` · `§2.5` item 4 · `P-GS-SM-3` |
| **3** | **POINTER-AGNOSTIC TERMINATION** — no `pointerIdOf` seam | no pointer identity is read; `pointerup` is **unconditionally terminal** for the session's own gesture; the session does not track which pointer a gesture belongs to | `M-9` · `F-9c` · `§2.4` items 1/3 · `P-GS-TP-2` · `I-11` |
| **4** | **A REPEAT `install` IS A NO-OP** — **first-config-wins**, returning `false`, with **no event-source call** | a second `install` on the same element does **not** double listeners and does **not** reconfigure them | `M-1` · `M-16` · `§2.3` item 1(b) · `§2.4` item 2 · `P-GS-SM-2` |
| **5** | **THE COUNT SEAM IS MODULE-LEVEL `stats()` / `gesture()`**, explicitly **NOT MCP-visible** | the count rows observe the session's own monotonic counters rather than a test-authored spy — the `ENGINE-PIN-PANE-INJECTION-SEAM` precedent (a deliberate, named production seam for testability, ruled 2026-09-27 on `U-ENGINE-PIN`) | `M-15` · `I-2` · `§5.3` item 10 · `P-GS-IM-6` · `P-GS-SM-3` |

**WHAT THIS DOES NOT CHANGE:** no row id, no attempt term, the `396` total, the seed, the caps, the strategy ids, the `§2.5` signature list, the export census or any prohibition. **`§7a.1`'s table is updated in place to record the adoption** (its `5 OPEN` reading is superseded by this block, kept visible as the filing state). **The delegation gate's ambiguity condition is now satisfied: `0` open questions.**

## 3b. The adversarial pass's disposition table — **the SHAPE this contract will be reconciled to**

**This table is EMPTY BY CONSTRUCTION at filing, and its VOCABULARY is fixed here** so an appended findings block needs **no renumbering and no new section**. **A row added later must use one of the statuses below, or the pass must define its new token IN THIS TABLE with a one-line meaning** — an undefined status word is what this shape exists to prevent.

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **CONFIRMED-FIXED** | a **host** finding, **fixed here + regression-tested** as a new `§3` row (or a new `R-*` row for a static finding) |
| **CONFIRMED-RULED** | a behaviour examined and ruled correct; the ruling recorded with its reason |
| **CONTRACT-AMENDED** | a seed that exposed a **gap in this spec** — the spec is amended with the old text kept visible as `SUPERSEDED`, and the row lands in `§3`/`§5.5.1` |
| **NOT-A-FINDING** | raised, examined, recorded with the reason |
| **OWED** | raised and **not yet resolved** — **the pass may not report done with an `OWED` row** |
| **OWED — HOST FIX** | a **host** finding whose fix is owed to the **Implementer** pass that follows, **red-first**, with its regression row owed to the **TestWriter**; **it may not be reported `DONE` until the fix lands, and its owner is named** |
| **OWED — TEST-SIDE** | a finding whose remedy is a **row** the TestWriter owns; **no `§5.5.1` statement, id, strategy id or attempt term may change for it** |
| **BLOCKING — SCOPE** | `A-9`/`A-11`/`A-12`/`A-13`/`A-8` returning positive: **the unit does not land** until the scope violation is removed |
| **HANDOFF** | a **package-class** finding → `docs/defects.md` + `docs/HANDOFF.md`; **the package is NEVER patched** |
| **PARKED-with-revisit-condition** | recorded, not fixed, with the condition that would reopen it and its owner |

### 3b.0 THE `§2.3` CLAUSE THIS RECORD RELIES ON — the DISCARD SHAPE (`DIS-1`)

**⟶ ADDED 2026-09-27 (THIS PASS — the gate-4 CLOSURE VERIFICATION ROUND 2's DISCARD-SHAPE clause, stated ONCE here as the clause of record; `§2.3` item 7.7 is NOT renumbered, item 7.8 is NOT created, and the clause's body is NOT written a second time into `§2.3` — the tombstone/pointer discipline `§2.3` item 7.3 already established for the item-7.7 MOVE is followed: ONE site carries the body.)** **THE CLAUSE'S ID IN THIS FILE IS `DIS-1` — a `§3b`-side clause id, NOT a new section number, NOT a `§5.5.1` register row and NOT a new result code.**

**`DIS-1` — THE DISCARD SHAPE: A `begin` WHOSE ATTEMPT'S OWN RECORD IS DISCARDED DURING THE ESTABLISHMENT ORDER BY CONSUMER CODE RUNNING INSIDE IT REFUSES `'disposed'`, EXACTLY AS THE MID-CALL DISPOSAL DOES.** **This is an EXTENSION of `§2.3` item 7.7 (`ADV-GS-30`) and it is stated in item 7.7's own terms; the as-filed item 7.7 text stands and is neither replaced nor renumbered.** **⟶ CROSS-REFERENCE CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (THE GATE-7 PROOFREADER PASS — the citation defect this record left open, now closed on both sides): §2.3 item 7.7 NOW CARRIES THIS DISCARD LIMB AS ITS SECOND LIMB, in a dated `⟶ EXTENDED` annotation at its own site, so the discard-shape row's citation of *"`§2.3` item 7.7's discard limb"* IS TRUE — before that annotation the limb was recorded ONLY here, at a `§3b`-side id a behaviour row cannot cite. The `§2.3`-side cell is a POINTER, not a second body: THIS cell (`§3b.0`, id `DIS-1`) remains the clause's ONE site of record, which is the discipline the note above and item 7.3's tombstone state. Item 7.7's number, its text, item 7.3's numbering and the `§2.3` clause list are all UNMOVED — no `7.8` was created — and no row id, attempt term, cap, the `396` total, the seed, a strategy id, the `12`-name census or the SEVEN-member code union moved.** **MEASURED, from the landed module: the liveness guard at the END of the establishment order is `ended || slot !== record` (`src/shared/gesture-session.ts`) — the `ended` limb is item 7.7's DISPOSAL shape (the session was disposed during the call), and the `slot !== record` limb is THIS clause's shape.** **THE SECOND SHAPE, stated exactly: the attempt's OWN record is discarded during the establishment order by CONSUMER CODE RUNNING INSIDE THAT ORDER — e.g. `cancel(element)` called from the consumer's own `onStart` hook — WITHOUT the session being disposed and WITHOUT `ended` becoming true.** **THE RULING, in `§2.3` item 7.7's words so a reader deriving a row takes it once: THAT ATTEMPT MUST NOT REPORT `{ok:true}` EITHER.** **It refuses `{ok:false, code:'disposed'}` — THE SAME REFUSAL THE DISPOSAL SHAPE USES, because the SEVEN-MEMBER CLOSED RESULT-CODE DOMAIN (`§2.1`'s `GestureCode`, `§4.4 S-9`) OFFERS NO MORE SPECIFIC MEMBER: the record that the call was establishing no longer exists, so a call may not report success on it, and no EIGHTH member is invented for it.** **The refused attempt consumes NOTHING: the gesture COUNTER is NOT consumed (`stats().gestures` stays at its pre-call value), the handle ID is NOT consumed (`stats().gestureId` stays at its pre-call value), `gesture()` stays `null`, and `stats().lastCode` reads `'disposed'` (`refuseBegin` sets it, and `§2.1`'s `lastCode` cell — "the LAST result code this session produced" — is the cell that DERIVES this reading; item 7.7 pins no `lastCode` cell of its own for this path).** **A RE-LEX OF THAT CODE FOR THE DISCARD-ONLY SHAPE (a `'no-gesture'` reading, or a `'stale'` reading) WOULD NEED ITS OWN GATE: it would be a change to a NORMATIVE clause and to the closed domain's assignment, so it is NOT made here — the reopening route is `§4.4 S-8`/`S-9`, with the architect ruling it.**

**STATE THE GUARD'S HONEST BOUND, because a defensive limb recorded as a driven behaviour would be an over-read.** **The discard limb is DEFENSIVE AND UNREACHABLE ON EVERY ESTABLISHED PATH — `slot` is assigned this attempt's own `record` inside `begin`, and only a terminal, a disposal, or this order's own re-entrancy can move it — so it exists for the re-entrant/hostile-consumer class (`A-6`) rather than for a path the contract expects.** **IT IS DRIVEN BY THE ROW THE PARALLEL TESTWRITER PASS ADDS (the discard-shape row in `tests/gesture-session.test.ts`), which is the row of record for it; THIS PASS WRITES NO TEST ROW and the row's own text is the TestWriter's.** **Disposition of the clause itself: `CONTRACT-AMENDED` (`§2.3` item 7.7 extended; no renumber, no new code, no count moved) — see `§3b.4`'s closing note, which is where this clause's disposition is recorded.**

### 3b.2 THE IMPLEMENTER'S JUDGMENT CALLS — the `FOUR` the closure rounds examined and RULED

**What these are.** While closing the gate-4 items, the Implementer made decisions the ledger/spec text leaves to the implementation rather than naming. **The two closure verification rounds examined FOUR of them as questions of contract conformance and RULED each.** The ruling vocabulary is this table's own (`CONFIRMED-RULED`, `CONTRACT-AMENDED`).

| # | The judgment call | Disposition | The ruling, with its reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **(a)** | **The `ended`-limb liveness check is performed TWICE — once at the TOP of `begin` (before the establishment checks) and once at the END of the establishment order, ABOVE the counter increment** | **`CONFIRMED-RULED`** | The top check is what makes `begin` on an ALREADY-disposed session refuse `'disposed'` without touching anything (item 7.3's *"`begin` reports `'disposed'`"*). The end re-check is what `ADV-GS-30` requires — **the disposal arrives from CONSUMER code that runs INSIDE the establishment order** (`capturePointer`, then `onStart`, per item 1(d)), so an early-only check could not observe it. **Neither check is redundant: they guard different windows, and `§2.3` item 8's cross-reference states the precedence the pair produces.** **No clause changed.** |
| **(b)** | **On a failed TRACKING attach the three attaches are ROLLED BACK (each successful `on` matched by an `off`), rather than left in place** | **`CONFIRMED-RULED`** | `I-4`'s declaration (*"at an installed, idle control the session holds EXACTLY ONE listener"*) and `I-5`'s baseline-exactness both bind here: a gesture that never establishes must not leave a tracking listener, and the rollback is the only shape that satisfies both while keeping the element **installed and re-establishable** (`§2.3` item 1(d)'s established-path property). **The refusal code is the EXISTING `'not-installed'`** — the record's own `slot` is cleared, so "not installed here as a gesture" is the honest reading with no eighth member. **No clause changed** (`ADV-GS-2` is the finding). |
| **(c)** | **The gesture counter and the id it feeds are incremented at the END of the establishment order — AFTER the tracking-attach check AND AFTER the `onStart` try/catch — while the record's own `id` is read at RECORD-BUILD time as `counters.gestures + 1`** | **`CONFIRMED-RULED`** | Three clauses hold together here and this is the only placement satisfying all three: `§2.1`'s `SessionStats.gestures` cell says *"successful `begin` calls"*; `§2.4` item 4 has the id start at `1` and increment on every **successful** `begin`; and `§2.3` item 1(d) requires a throwing-`onStart` attempt to consume **neither** the counter **nor** the id. Building the record's id as `counters.gestures + 1` (rather than consuming a value) is what makes the two requirements true together — **ids stay consecutive (`1, 2, 3…`) for successful gestures and an aborted attempt's unused id is not spent.** **The ruling's own boundary, stated: this is a RULING about the OBSERVABLE (`gestures`/`gestureId`/the retry's id), so a different internal placement that yields the same three observables would conform — this one is ruled correct, not mandated.** |
| **(d)** | **An opt-in control's capture call FIRES before `onStart`, and the session NEVER RELEASES it — including when the establishment subsequently fails** | **`CONFIRMED-RULED` (the no-release half)**, with the residual recorded as **`PARKED-with-revisit-condition`** | The capture call's POSITION is item 6(b)'s (**after the tracking attaches, before `onStart`**), so it precedes a throwing `onStart`; and **item 6(c)'s rule governs what happens next — *"no release call is invented"***. **The session therefore does not release, and this contract does not require a release.** **The residual, recorded honestly rather than smoothed over: the state is a RECORDED GAP IN THE CAPTURE LIFECYCLE, not a contract breach** — **it is PARKED at `§2.3` item 6(c)'s own dated note, whose revisit condition is a composing unit (`U-GUTTER` `E3` / `U-RELOCATE` `E4`) that needs the release, or a real-renderer observation of a stuck capture; a NEW clause would change item 6's call counts and therefore MAY NOT be landed by re-reading item 6.** |

**Judgment-call arithmetic: `4` calls · `3` `CONFIRMED-RULED` (`(a)`, `(b)`, `(c)`) · `1` `CONFIRMED-RULED` with a `PARKED-with-revisit-condition` residual (`(d)`) · `0` `OWED` · `0` module change required by any of them beyond the findings `§3b.4` records.**

### 3b.3 THE GREEN-TIME TEST REPAIRS — each ruled **TEST-side**, and the load-bearing question answered

**What these are.** The red→green cycles landed **NINE test-side repairs** beyond the plain row additions. **Each is ruled `TEST-side`, and for each the pass verified the load-bearing question: NO CONTRACT-CONFORMING MODULE COULD HAVE SATISFIED THE OLD ROW.** **A repair that made a row pass by WEAKENING it would be the never-weaken-a-clause violation; these are the opposite class — the ROW was wrong about the contract, or the row asserted a fact its own drive did not produce, and no module was bent for any of them.** *(The disposition word used here, `TEST-side`, maps onto this table's vocabulary as `OWED — TEST-SIDE`'s remedy class, now LANDED; see `§3b.4` for the per-finding owners.)*

| # | The repaired row | What was wrong with it AS FILED | Why the OLD row could not carry its claim | Finding |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | **`F-8`'s failed-attach limb** (`§3.2`) | its fixture asserted the **VIOLATION AS ITS EXPECTATION**: the failed start attach's ledger entry was asserted as the expected behaviour, so the row *encoded* the defect the pass then had to fix | the row asserted a behaviour the CONTRACT DOES NOT HAVE (`§2.4` item 6: a failed `on` leaves **no ledger entry**), so a conforming module **FAILED** it and the non-conforming one passed — the row was inverted | **`ADV-GS-1`** |
| **2** | **`P-GS-IM-1`'s declared-vs-distinct triple** (`§5.5.1`) | its *"`15` distinct inputs"* figure was **wrong or unreproducible** against the table's own configurations | the figure did not follow from the row's own data, so the cell could be neither confirmed nor falsified from the file — **the row now DERIVES the figure** rather than printing a literal | **`ADV-GS-18`/`-19`/`-20`** (and its **`ADV-GS-36`** reconciliation) |
| **3** | **`P-GS-IM-5`'s distinct figure** (`§5.5.1`) | the **declared-vs-distinct** figure was wrong or unreproducible for the flag-value table | as (2): a printed literal that its own drive does not produce is not a row claim — **the row now DERIVES it** | **`ADV-GS-19`** |
| **4** | **`P-GS-IM-6`'s distinct figure** (`§5.5.1`) | as (3), for the session-shape × gesture-state × operation table | as (2) — **the row now DERIVES it** | **`ADV-GS-20`** |
| **5** | **`M-18`'s write instrument** (`§3.1`) | its instrument **covered `3` of the families it claimed** — so the *"no UI content is authored"* claim rested on a spy triple that could not see the other families — **and its change-set limb was VACUOUS** | the row's own claim was broader than the instrument that made it falsifiable, and a vacuous limb cannot fail: **no module — conforming or not — could be distinguished by it.** **The repair widens the surface to EVERY `R-12` family and replaces the vacuous change-set limb with a REAL op census** | **`ADV-GS-23`** |
| **6** | **`PRE-4` and `P-GS-TP-1`'s limbs** (`§5.5.1`/harness) | **VACUOUS and DUPLICATE limbs** — limbs that re-asserted a claim already driven, or that asserted nothing their own data could refute | a vacuous limb is not a row (it can never fail), and a duplicate limb is the `ADV-CN-6` class applied to tests: **the pair added no evidence while appearing to.** **The repair removes the vacuous/duplicated limbs and keeps the substantive ones** | **`ADV-GS-24`** |
| **7** | **The bounded-set assertion** (`§5.5.1`/harness) | it was an **unfalsifiable LITERAL-vs-LITERAL comparison** that **printed `FOUR` where the spec's register prints `FIVE`** | a literal compared against another literal in the same file proves nothing about the register, and the `FOUR`-vs-`FIVE` disagreement meant the row and the spec had already drifted. **The repair DERIVES the set from the register table**, so the assertion moves when the marking moves | **`ADV-GS-32`** (the `ADV-GS-25` marking is its cause) |
| **8** | **A row's stale prose figure** (`§3.2`-area row prose) | its prose cited the **SUPERSEDED spec figure `15`** — a figure this spec itself had already corrected | the row's prose disagreed with the cells it was derived from: a citation of superseded text is the staleness class `AGENTS.md` item 10d exists to catch. **The repair cites the ruled figures** | **`ADV-GS-33`** |
| **9** | **The `M-4`-area row and the `less 2` message** (the row that read `gesture().commits`) | it demanded a reading the surface **cannot produce** (`gesture().commits === 1` after a terminal) **and** its message carried a **stale *"less 2"*** arithmetic | the terminal DISCARDS the active record, so `gesture()` is `null` after it (`§2.4` item 8, `M-17`): **no conforming module could satisfy the old row's read**, and its message printed arithmetic the spec had superseded. **The repair reads `stats().commits` + the commit sink + `handle.outcome`** (exactly what `§0` `ADV-GS-35`/`M-4` now require) **and drops the stale message** | **`ADV-GS-35`** + **`ADV-GS-27`** |

**Repair arithmetic: `9` repairs · `9` ruled `TEST-side` (LANDED) · `0` modules bent for any of them · `0` clauses weakened.** **The pass's verification, stated once for all nine: in every case the old row either asserted the defect as its expectation (repair 1), printed a figure its own drive did not produce (repairs 2–4), claimed more than its instrument could see or asserted nothing at all (repairs 5–7), cited superseded text (repairs 8–9).** **NONE of the nine changed a `§5.5.1` statement, a row id, a strategy id or an attempt term — the register's numbers are UNMOVED, exactly as the `OWED — TEST-SIDE` status word requires.**
